⛔ **`**State:**` NOT `- [ ]`, deliberately — matching every 13.8x sibling.** `plan-lint` reduces a `13.8g-C`-style id to `13.8`, so a checkbox here becomes a SECOND checkbox on the parent arc's task (caught by `plan-lint` FAIL `L1812` when this was first written with one). **Same mechanism as the 9.10-D defect recorded at `### 9.41`** — an id with a letter suffix resolves to its numeric parent, so a brief citing it would lint green against the wrong task. The arc's sub-tasks all use `**State:**` for exactly this reason; do not "fix" it into a checkbox.
⛔ **ESTABLISHED FROM SOURCE 2026-07-31 (worker at 13.8g-B Step 2.5; orchestrator re-verified every citation before recording): a meeting extraction field can NEVER be array-valued, and it is DELIBERATE.** `apps/worker/src/composition/meeting-extraction.ts:71-73` `isPrimitiveOrTbd` admits `string | number | boolean` only, and its own comment at `:70` states the intent outright — *"NOT an object/array/function (**the model must not emit a structured value**)."* `createMeetingExtractionSchemaGate` (`:96-118`) rejects the **ENTIRE** extraction with `schema_rejected` on the first non-primitive field value — not per-field — and it is wired at `buildActivities.ts:518` into `createValidateActivity`, **upstream of `buildOutputs.ts`**. ⇒ **a `ValidatedExtraction` reaching `buildOutputs` cannot carry an array-valued field, by construction.** The frozen `sow:agent-extraction` schema agrees (`packages/contracts/src/models/agent-extraction.ts:70-75`, same union, no array member) though it is not the gate wired today.
⛔ **AND THREE INDEPENDENT AUTHORS ASSUMED THE OPPOSITE — that is the actual finding, not the gate itself.** `packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/attendee-refs.ts:173` (`normalizeAttendees` returns empty unless `Array.isArray`) · `packages/workflows/src/activities/projections/meetingOutputs.ts:159-160` (an `Array.isArray(value) ? … : String(value)` render branch) · `.../noteSlug.ts:37-44` (`neutralizeFrontmatterValue`'s array handling, whose own comment says it is *"not relying on the extraction schema's flat-string[] shape"*). **Two of those branches are dead code today and the third silently no-ops the whole 13.8g arc.** ⇒ **the two halves of the codebase hold contradictory beliefs about the same field, and one half must move.**
⛔ **DECIDED 2026-07-31 — LEAD RULING, owner-delegated as "the most architecturally correct answer." Direction (ii), REFINED; ⛔ do NOT implement (ii) as it was written below.** **The refinement is load-bearing: do NOT globally widen `isPrimitiveOrTbd`** — it is generic across every `ExtractionField`, so widening it admits arrays for **all** fields and flips a rule-2 surface from default-closed to default-open. ⭐ **That defect was in the ORCHESTRATOR's own framing of option (ii) and the lead caught it.** **Instead — per-field, schema-declared, default-CLOSED list support:** (1) list-ness is a property of the **FIELD's declaration in `sow:agent-extraction`**, not of the gate; an undeclared field stays scalar-only and is rejected exactly as today; (2) a declared list is **`string[]` only** — elements scalar-validated, **length-capped**, **nesting rejected**; objects/functions stay forbidden everywhere, which is what `meeting-extraction.ts:70`'s comment actually guards; (3) **TWO independent mechanisms** — Zod **and** the generated JSON Schema, so ajv holds even if the TS type is bypassed (13.18/§DEC-CANDGATE precedent; a compile-time claim over runtime-untrusted bytes is L103's inverse); (4) **contract-first sequencing** — `packages/contracts` (schema + snapshot) → worker (gate) → knowledge (⭐ the normalizer should need **NO** change; if it does, that is a **finding**); (5) **`security-reviewer` MANDATORY** on the gate slice — rule 2; (6) **delete nothing** — the three branches become live, which is the point.
⚠ **CALL IT A RELAXATION (orchestrator pushback, 2026-07-31, accepted as a correction to the ruling's framing).** The ruling described this as *"not relaxing the gate — making its prohibition precise."* **That is accurate about the MECHANISM and soft about the DIRECTION:** after this change the gate admits a value shape it previously rejected. Narrow-and-governed does not mean not-a-relaxation. ⇒ **the honest sentence is "a SCOPED, DECLARED, DEFAULT-CLOSED RELAXATION of a rule-2 gate."** **Why the word matters practically: it sets the review posture.** A reviewer told *"a prohibition was made precise"* checks the new rule is well-formed; a reviewer told *"a candidate-data gate was relaxed"* asks **what can now get through that could not before, and whether the opt-in itself can be forged** — and only the second reviewer asks whether a candidate can declare its own list-ness. On a rule-2 surface we want the second reviewer.
⛔ **RULED 2026-07-31 (lead, on orchestrator pushback — the gap was real): DECLARE BOTH `attendees` AND `decisions`.** ⭐ **And the load-bearing half is NOT which fields — it is that LIST-NESS HAS EXACTLY ONE SOURCE.** The declaration lives in one place; the gate reads it; consumers read it. ⛔ **No consumer may hand-assume via a local `Array.isArray` against its own belief — that divergence IS the bug, and re-creating it per-consumer merely relocates it.** ⭐ **Establish whether a cheap test can pin the CORRESPONDENCE — declared-as-list ⟺ consumed-as-list — and if one exists, write it: that is what prevents instance #3, and it is the make-it-unrepresentable pattern aimed at the DISAGREEMENT rather than at either half. If no cheap pin exists, SAY SO in the Step-9 flags** — *"we couldn't"* is a fine answer; an unmentioned gap is not. **State the criterion in-code so the next list-shaped field is not a judgement call.** ⛔ **ALSO RULED — the forgery question is a MANDATORY security-review item answered BY CONSTRUCTION, not by inspection: A CANDIDATE MUST NOT BE ABLE TO DECLARE ITS OWN LIST-NESS.** The schema declares; the payload never does. **Any path where model output influences which fields are treated as lists is a rule-2 BREACH and a Finding to the lead, not a design detail.** ⭐ Leg A's design answers it structurally — JSON Schema's `properties` (declared keys) over `additionalProperties` (scalar-only rest) is default-closed by precedence, and **a payload cannot add itself to `properties`**. ⛔ **HAZARD carried into leg A's brief:** `propertyNames` (the prototype-pollution guard) is emitted from `z.record`'s KEY schema, and `.object().catchall()` has no key-schema slot — the container swap can **silently drop a guard a prior security review added** (`agent-extraction.ts:40-47`). **Verify it survives by READING the regenerated JSON; if it cannot, ESCALATE rather than trading one guard for the other.**
⛔ **THE FAILURE MODE WHOEVER REVIEWS LEG A MUST BE TOLD, because it is INVISIBLE IN A GREEN DIFF: SOMEONE MEETS THREE RED TESTS AND "FIXES" THEM BY WEAKENING THE ASSERTION.** Established at leg A's Step 2.5 by driving real Zod + real ajv: the proposed `.object().catchall()` container drops **three** guards, not the one the brief named — (1) ajv's `propertyNames` vanishes (no key-schema slot); (2) **Zod's own key check vanishes too** (`z.record(FIELD_NAME,…)` enforces key names during `.parse()`; a catchall bucket validates VALUES only); (3) ⭐ **`.catchall()` SILENTLY DROPS a genuine `__proto__` own-key during Zod's internal reconstruction** — it neither fails nor survives, and it happens too early for any post-parse `.superRefine()` to see. **That is the exact L3 ajv↔Zod parity gap `agent-extraction.ts:40-47` was written to close, re-opened by the container swap.** ⚠ **BUT THE EXISTING GUARD CATCHES ALL THREE, LOUDLY** — `test/models/agent-extraction.test.ts:210-214` asserts `safeParse(...).success === false` for `__proto__`/`constructor`/`prototype`, so a naive migration turns **three tests RED**. ⭐ **A prior security review's guard is doing exactly its job; the DROP is silent and that test is what converts the silence into noise.** ⇒ **The danger is therefore NOT the guard missing it — it is a reviewer seeing three reds on a "schema shape" slice and treating them as fixture churn.** ⛔ **Any weakening of those three assertions is a rule-2 regression, not a test fix.** *(recorded durably 2026-07-31 at the lead's instruction, because it had existed only in session-scoped task metadata that dies at respawn — contracts L51)*
⭐ **LEG A DONE** · `5eaf33f5` · 2026-07-31 (contract; brief `docs/briefs/238-13.8g-C-A-declare-list-valued-extraction-fields.md` `@96f5542d`) — `LIST_VALUED_EXTRACTION_FIELDS` = exactly `attendees`/`decisions`, `string[]` capped at 200, elements validated, nesting rejected; scalar/TBD still legal everywhere; every other field keeps scalar-only rejection. 803 tests green, repo-wide typecheck across all 11 packages confirming **zero blast radius outside `packages/contracts`**. `security-reviewer` **0 critical/high/medium**, 3 low (all defer, all recorded) — and it ran **adversarially**, executing disposable probes reproducing each hazard rather than reading prose.
⛔ **THE LEAD'S TWO EVIDENCE CHECKS — BOTH AFFIRMATIVE, ANSWERED BY EXECUTION AND RECORDED HERE so no future reader re-derives them** (the pre-ruling's whole value was *the guards survive*, and ⭐ **a clean reviewer pass says nobody found a problem — it does NOT say the specific property that was ruled for got checked**; contracts **L118** aimed at a ruling rather than a report): **(1) `propertyNames` verified present in the REGENERATED JSON by READING THE EMITTED FILE, not inferred from what Zod should produce** — `packages/contracts/schemas/agent-extraction.schema.json:105-107` carries `"propertyNames": {"pattern": "^(?!(?:__proto__|prototype|constructor)$)"}`. Established **three independent ways**: contract's test asserts the *generated schema's actual content*, the security review compiled a **fresh ajv** and confirmed rejection behaviourally, and the orchestrator **read the file directly** at tick time. ⚠ Reading the artifact rather than reasoning about the emission is the check *because the ajv↔Zod parity gap IS what that guard closes.* **(2) The three reserved-key assertions are BYTE-UNCHANGED** — `test/models/agent-extraction.test.ts:212-214`, still `safeParse(raw("__proto__"|"constructor"|"prototype")).success === false`, and `git diff 5eaf33f5~1 5eaf33f5` touches **zero lines** in that block. ⭐ **This was the sharper risk and it is now closed with evidence: *someone meets three red tests and "fixes" them by weakening the assertion* — a passing test that was edited to pass looks identical to success in a green suite.** ⛔ **Any future weakening of those three assertions is a rule-2 REGRESSION, not a test fix.**
⭐ **TWO ORCHESTRATOR RULINGS APPLIED IN-SLICE:** `.passthrough()`/`unknownKeys` named in `emit.ts` as an **explicitly uncovered sibling** (L96 — a classifying guard must name the vectors it cannot classify, or a comment implying coverage becomes the defect); and `AgentExtractionCandidateField` **renamed** `…ScalarField` — ⭐ **a proxy/property mismatch at the TYPE level (L118): the name reported "a candidate field," the type measured "a scalar candidate field."** Accurate before this slice, silently false after. ⛔ A speculatively-widened sibling was **declined** — leg B exports what it needs when it needs it. ⚠ **Leg A's Step-2.5 ALSO produced a verified three-part fix that preserves every guard** (`.catchall()` container **+** a `preprocess` key guard that sees the raw input before Zod's reconstruction **+** an `emitJsonSchema` `postProcess` rule restoring `propertyNames` for any object with a real catchall) — ⇒ **the lead's "if the container cannot carry the guards, the container is wrong" pre-ruling was never put to the test; no guard was traded.** **Legs: A contracts (in flight) → B worker (the gate reads the declaration) → C knowledge (expected NO change; ⚠ if the normalizer DOES need changing that is a FINDING against the ruling, surfaced loudly).** ⚠ Leg C's shape is settled at leg A's **Step-2.5**, not at its commit — so knowledge unblocks sooner than a strict A→B→C reading implies.
⭐ **LEG C DONE** · `b0319823` · 2026-07-31 · test-only · brief `docs/briefs/240-13.8g-C-C-verify-normalizeattendees-and-pin-correspondence.md` (`@f742a5ad`) — the confirmed-no-change premise was re-verified independently from source (not taken on the brief's word): `normalizeAttendees` already `Array.isArray`-gates at entry and per-element string-validates, matching leg A's declared shape exactly, built dormant ahead of its own wiring. Closes the ruling's one still-open obligation with a correspondence pin (`attendees_is_declared_list_valued_in_the_shared_schema`, mutation-verified via a swapped checked literal — never the real declared array, out of contract-implementer's territory) — banked as **contracts L119**. Cap-compatibility across three independent stages (contracts' 200-item schema-level reject · knowledge's 2000-entry defensive slice · knowledge's 200-ref output cap) confirmed non-conflicting from source. No production code change; `no_production_caller` dormancy pin unmodified, re-run green. **Remaining: leg B (the worker gate reading the declaration) is the only open leg of 13.8g-C.**
⛔ **SUPERSEDED — the question below was recorded as open and has since been RULED (above). ⭐ **THE ORIGINAL TWO-SIDED FRAMING, preserved because the ruling's reasoning only makes sense against it:** **(i)** keep the gate scalar-only (it is deliberate, and "the model must not emit a structured value" is a defensible candidate-data posture) and adopt an explicit **delimited-string convention**, with parsing owned by `normalizeAttendees` — ⛔ **and by NOTHING ELSE** (a second splitter beside it is contracts **L39**/**L61**: a naive `split(",")` mangles `"Smith, Jane" <jane@acme.com>`, a real shape, and forks a parser for untrusted free text that one module was carefully built to own); **or (ii)** widen the gate to admit an array **of scalars** for list-valued fields, which makes all three existing branches live and correct and leaves `normalizeAttendees` untouched — but relaxes a deliberate rule-2 structural constraint and needs its own security look. ⛔ **Do NOT resolve this inside a wiring slice**, and do not let it be settled by whichever consumer is being edited at the time — that is how the disagreement got here.
**Files (direction-dependent):** (i) `packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/attendee-refs.ts` + the extraction prompt/contract naming the delimiter — **knowledge territory** · (ii) `apps/worker/src/composition/meeting-extraction.ts` + `packages/contracts/src/models/agent-extraction.ts` + snapshots — **worker + contract, a frozen-contract round**. Either way the two dead array branches are reconciled rather than left asserting a shape that cannot arrive.
**Done-when:** the direction is chosen and recorded; exactly ONE parser owns attendee-string splitting if (i); the gate's scalar-only comment is either still true or amended if (ii); 13.8g-B's characterization pin (*"a realistic schema-gated scalar yields ZERO refs"*) is **replaced by a pin proving refs actually reach the rewrite input**; and no consumer retains an array branch that cannot fire. *(origin: 2026-07-31 13.8g-B Step 2.5, worker-implementer — found by establishing the real shape rather than accepting the brief's premise; the orchestrator's brief had assumed the value was merely a different SHAPE, not a structurally impossible one)*

#### 13.8h — Cap the MODEL-supplied `candidate.entityRefs` fan-out in `planSynthesis` (unbounded-read vector)
**State:** DONE · `bed423cb` · 2026-07-29 — ⛔ **this arming precondition is CLOSED (see §ARM-RESEARCH item 8)** · **Kind:** build · **Spec:** §6 (KN-10) · **Depends:** 13.8c (`461a0186`) · **Blocks:** the 13.8e/13.8f arming crossing
⛔ **DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE FROM THIS TASK'S OWN `Done-when` — READ BEFORE "FIXING" IT.** The Done-when below says *"keep the cap a single shared constant so a caller-side and planner-side cap can't drift."* **That was overridden on purpose** (orchestrator ruling at Step 2.5, 2026-07-29): the shipped cap is `MAX_MODEL_ENTITY_REFS`, an **INDEPENDENT** constant in `planner.ts`, deliberately NOT coupled to `meeting-rewrite.ts`'s `MAX_ENTITY_REFS`. **Reason:** single-sourcing (L5/L37) governs literals that must *agree*; these must not. `MAX_ENTITY_REFS` bounds **deterministic input the meeting path owns**; `MAX_MODEL_ENTITY_REFS` bounds **adversarial model output**. Coupled, a future tuning of one silently retunes the other's **threat model** — and a circular import (`meeting-rewrite` → `planner`) made sharing impossible anyway. Both are 200 today so nothing changed behaviourally, and the in-code comment states the decoupling. ⛔ **Do not "helpfully" single-source them.**
⭐ **Surfaced, not silently sliced:** `SynthesisOutcome.entityRefsTruncated` carries the dropped **count** (rule 7 — a count, never a ref/name/path), hoisted above the `try` so a later fault cannot discard it (mirroring 13.8m-A's refusals accumulator — the first time this round that invariant was pinned *before* a mutation pass found it missing). The truncation is head-first and deterministic, pinned by asserting the **actual GBrain call count** rather than the reported number — the property, not the mechanism (L70).
**Files:** `packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/planner.ts` (`collectEntities` — resolves `candidate.entityRefs` with no cap) · `test/synthesis-planner.test.ts`
`planSynthesis` resolves the MODEL-supplied `candidate.entityRefs` with **no cap**, so a degenerate REASON output (a model emitting hundreds/thousands of refs) drives an **unbounded sequential GBrain read loop** — a self-inflicted resource-exhaustion path one layer BELOW the deterministic caps 13.8d/13.8f-A enforce on the inputs they own. Those callers cannot fix it: they bound their own `input.entityRefs`, not the planner's model-derived set. Currently invisible because the planner is dormant — which is exactly why it must be closed BEFORE the synthesis path arms, not after. Cap the model-derived fan-out (mirror `MAX_ENTITY_REFS`), surface the truncation rather than dropping silently, and keep the cap a single shared constant so a caller-side and planner-side cap can't drift. **Done-when:** a degenerate candidate with refs beyond the cap issues a bounded number of GBrain reads, the truncation is observable (not a silent slice), and the cap constant is shared with the deterministic callers. *(origin: 2026-07-26 13.8f-A Step-9 flag, knowledge — reviewer-surfaced)*

#### 13.8j — Namespace entity stub paths so untrusted names cannot collide with KN-12 structural surfaces
**State:** DONE · `6dc6c34f` · 2026-07-26 (per-kind namespace `people/`/`projects/`/`concepts/` + `entities/` fail-safe for a malformed model-supplied kind; derivation lives ONCE in `stubNotePathFor`; `ReadonlyMap` not an object literal, after the first fix let `__proto__`/`toString` resolve through the prototype chain back to the vault root — contracts L65; 16 hostile-kind vectors pinned; exact-match, no normalization) · **Kind:** build · **Spec:** §6 (KN-10 grounding, KN-12 structural parity), safety rule 1 · **Depends:** 13.8c `461a0186`, 13.8f-A `d723a4fc`, 13.8g-A · **Blocks:** the 13.8e/13.8f arming crossing
**Files:** `packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/entity-resolver.ts` (shared path derivation) · `planner.ts:288` · `meeting-rewrite.ts:185` · tests
Entity stub paths are minted at the VAULT ROOT as `${proposedSlug}.md` at **two** independent call sites — `meeting-rewrite.ts:185` AND `planner.ts:288` (the orchestrator's verification found the second; the Step-9 report named only the first, so **the SOURCE-ingestion path carries it too**, and that path is already bound dormant by 13.8d `172f9aed`). So an entity named `Index`/`Log`/`README` mints `index.md`/`log.md` — the KnowledgeWriter-owned KN-12 structural surfaces (navigation catalog + append-only op-log). This defeats a deliberate design decision: `MeetingRewriteDeps` omits the `structural` port precisely so a meeting cannot touch those files, and this reaches them by another door. Attacker-reachable since 13.8g-A began feeding entity names from untrusted attendee strings. Traversal itself is clean (`entitySlug` collapses `../../etc/passwd` → `etc-passwd`); the exposure is COLLISION, not escape. **⛔ Fix by NAMESPACE (`people/`/`projects/`/`concepts/` by `EntityKind`), NOT a reserved-slug denylist** — enumeration is structurally unwinnable and this codebase has paid for it twice (the shadow-env key set needed 3 re-grounds and still missed a switch, worker L72; the settings-injection FIELD enumeration was retired for a presence-degrade, 18.39-B). Derive the path in ONE shared place so both consumers inherit the namespace by construction — the duplication is what enabled the defect. **Done-when:** an `Index`/`Log`-named entity cannot mint a root structural file via EITHER path, stub paths are namespaced per kind, resolved (non-stub) paths are unchanged, and no inline `${...proposedSlug}.md` construction remains. *(origin: 2026-07-26 13.8g-A Step-9 flag, knowledge — both reviewers; scope widened by orchestrator verification)*

#### 13.8k — INVARIANT: every path entering `groundedPaths` is shape-validated, whoever produced it
**State:** DONE · `3acb2e0b` · 2026-07-26 (shared `admitGroundedPath` as the single admission point; resolver calls it so every consumer inherits it; owned-surface set DERIVED from exported `structural-files.ts` constants; refusal withholds with a code-only reason, never sanitizes; resolved paths byte-identical. 3 review catches fixed in-slice incl. an already-grounded regression that minted a NoteCreate over a confirmed note, and a 4-read TOCTOU on `meetingNotePath`; 2 of its own new pins were unsound and are now mutation-verified) — ⛔ **arming precondition for the synthesis path** · **Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §6 (KN-10 grounding, KN-12), §5 (WS-8), safety rule 1 · **Depends:** 13.8j · **Blocks:** the 13.8e/13.8f arming crossing
**Files:** `packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/entity-resolver.ts` (the `resolved` branch / candidate-row admission) · tests
13.8j closed the STUB-minting door to the writer-owned KN-12 structural surfaces by namespacing. The **`resolved` branch is a third door it deliberately did not touch:** `resolveEntity` returns `candidate.path` **VERBATIM** from the GBrain read, shape-guarded only as a non-empty string — so a poisoned candidate row carrying `path: "index.md"` (or `log.md`, or a `Logs/<date>.md`) plus a faithfully-matching title RESOLVES to that path, lands in `groundedPaths`, and the model may then patch it. 13.8j is CORRECT not to re-path resolved notes (re-pathing would silently break every grounding match — pinned by `resolved_paths_unchanged`), so the fix belongs at the resolver's admission boundary instead: reject/withhold a candidate row whose `path` is shape-invalid or targets a writer-owned structural surface, rather than sanitizing it after the fact. Note the general shape — **13.8j fixed the instances of a CONSTRUCTION; this is a different route to the same INVARIANT violation** (contracts L64: enumerate the concept, not the construction). **⚠ SCOPED AS AN INVARIANT, deliberately (2026-07-26, on the implementer's correction):** the first phrasing of this task — "add a path-shape guard on candidate rows at the resolver boundary" — was itself a CONSTRUCTION/location phrasing, i.e. the same L64 error one layer up: it would be satisfied by guarding the resolver while leaving any future producer free to put an unvalidated path into `groundedPaths` by another route. The requirement is the INVARIANT: **every path that enters `groundedPaths` is shape-validated, regardless of which code produced it** — resolved candidate rows today, and any future producer by construction. Implement it where that invariant can be enforced once (the point where paths enter the grounded set), not at each known producer. **THREE routes to this one invariant are now known** (a strong argument that the invariant, not the guard site, is the right unit): (i) stub minting — closed by 13.8j's namespace construction; (ii) RESOLVED candidate rows — `candidate.path` verbatim from the GBrain read (the reported route); (iii) **`meetingNotePath`** — caller-supplied and SEEDS the grounded set (`meeting-rewrite.ts:172`), so a meeting note "at" `index.md` lets the model patch the navigation catalog; found at Step 2.5 by applying the invariant framing, enumerated by neither the Finding nor the brief. ⚠ Requires a WRITE to `markdown-vault/structural-files.ts`: it exports no owned-surface constants today (index path is a caller arg; `logsDir`/`pointerPath` are inline defaults), so the guard has nothing to derive from until `STRUCTURAL_INDEX_PATH`/`STRUCTURAL_LOG_POINTER_PATH`/`STRUCTURAL_LOGS_DIR` are exported and consumed by the builders as their defaults. Residual: those being DEFAULTS means a caller could pass a custom path the guard wouldn't know — closed by a structural pin that no production call site does, rather than by removing configurability. **Done-when:** the invariant holds and is pinned — a path targeting `index.md`/`log.md`/`Logs/…` or otherwise shape-invalid cannot enter `groundedPaths` from ANY producer; legitimate resolved paths are untouched (grounding matches still work); and the enforcement lives in ONE place with a structural pin that a second, unguarded entry point would fail. *(origin: 2026-07-26 13.8j Step-9 Finding, knowledge — orchestrator scoping miss in brief 206)*

#### 13.8l — The SOURCE path has NO grounded set: apply `admitGroundedPath` to plan targets (the FOURTH door)
**State:** DONE · `89eea8df` · 2026-07-28 (`admitPlanMutations` applied ONCE at `ingest-rewrite.ts:132-139`, placed so `touchedNotePaths` and `mergeStructural` both inherit it from the single `semantic` read — no second enforcement point; `deps.structural` deliberately merged in AFTER admission because its index.md/log.md targets ARE KN-12 parity) · **Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §6 (KN-10/KN-12), safety rule 1 · **Depends:** 13.8k · **Blocks:** the synthesis arming crossing
**Files:** `packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/ingest-rewrite.ts` / the 13.8d source path · `synthesis/grounded-path.ts` (reuse, do not re-derive) · tests
13.8k established the invariant on the MEETING path and closed three routes. The **source** path has **no grounded set at all**: `planSynthesis` output reaches the `KnowledgeMutationPlan` and `touchedNotePaths` unguarded, so a model-proposed `patches:[{path:"index.md"}]` is stopped only by the worker adapter's realpath containment — which prevents **escape** but NOT **collision** with a writer-owned surface. So this is a fourth door to the same invariant, on the path that is already bound (dormant) by 13.8d. The implementer correctly scoped 13.8k's module header to say the invariant holds on the meeting path only, rather than letting an unqualified sentence over-promise. Fix by applying the existing `admitGroundedPath` to the source path's plan targets — reuse the single admission point, do not add a second. **Done-when:** a model-proposed patch/create targeting a writer-owned structural surface cannot reach the source path's KMP or `touchedNotePaths`, enforced through the SAME admission function, with the one-enforcement-point pin extended to cover it. *(origin: 2026-07-26 13.8k Step-9 Finding, knowledge)*

#### 13.8m — Refusals reach nobody: a poisoned-row attack is byte-identical to a benign empty run
**State:** PARTIAL · landed: **13.8m-A** `89eea8df` — the code-only producer channel on the SOURCE receipt (`IngestRewriteReceipt.refusals`, `ingest-rewrite.ts:97`; accumulator hoisted above the `try` at `:115` so a fault AFTER admission cannot discard what was already refused, and present on the fail-safe `empty()` receipt at `:116`); **13.8m-B** ✅ `2c03b3af` 2026-07-29 — the worker consumer, SOURCE path (brief 214 `@324188d6`): `LivingVaultRewrite`'s return widened with an optional `refusals`, an optional best-effort `recordRefusals` sink on `LivingVaultAdapterDeps`, fired **once per run BEFORE root resolution/containment** so a refused-**then**-rejected run still surfaces its codes (workspace + reason codes only, rule 7). A declined quality nit is also recorded: `unbound_sink_is_byte_equivalent` does not tightly pin the `typeof sink !== "function"` guard, because the outer try/catch would make any tighter pin pass for the wrong reason (L79) — declined and disclosed rather than papered over. **13.8m-C ✅ DONE** `71b0e437` 2026-07-29 (brief 226 `@d49c30de`) — the MEETING path's producer half. `MeetingRewriteReceipt.refusals` added **REQUIRED** (⚠ deliberately mirroring **A**, not B: B's optional worker seam was a recorded bound taken to keep 13 containment fakes valid, and copying the newer sibling would have propagated it to a second path — contracts **L105**'s first live application; zero breakage, the receipt has no consumers outside its module + test). Accumulator **hoisted above the `try`**, `empty` converted to a **closure** so all three fail-safe exits carry accumulated refusals, code-only per rule 7. Mutation-verified (hardcoded empty `refusals` in the `catch` ⇒ exactly one test RED, 24 green, reverted); required-field pin proven via **`TS2578`**, not vitest (which does not typecheck test files). Reviewers: security 0 findings, code-quality 1 LOW applied. ⭐ **The brief's premise was WRONG and the implementer corrected it:** the real discard point is `resolveEntity`'s internal `withheld(reason)` (grounded-path route 2), **not** this module's `admitInto` call — whose three sites are provably unreachable-to-fail, so threading them would have been the L75 "guard never observed to fail" shape.
⛔ **Remaining:** the MEETING-path **consumer** (the 13.8m-B analogue). **`§ARM-RESEARCH` precondition (7) is NOT closed** — after C, the meeting path has a populated channel and no reader. **Do NOT read 13.8m-B or -C as "refusals now reach the operator"**; that stays SOURCE-path-only.
⛔ **NOT DISPATCHABLE TO knowledge — established 2026-07-30. It is the THIRD step of three and the first two are unbuilt, both of them WORKER work.** The entity-grounded meeting pipeline has **zero production callers**: `MeetingRewriteInput`/`Deps`/`Receipt` and `normalizeAttendees` occur only inside `packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/*` and their own tests — **zero** in `apps/worker` or `packages/workflows` — and `meeting-rewrite.test.ts:421-427`'s `no_production_caller` pin asserts exactly that. Prerequisites, both OPEN: **(1) 13.8f-B** (`:1801`) must make anything call `rewriteVaultForMeeting` at all and fold the receipt into the meeting KMP, which today hardcodes `linkMutations: []` at `packages/workflows/src/activities/buildOutputs.ts:242` — **there is no meeting-path analogue of `apps/worker/src/composition/living-vault.ts`**; **(2) 13.8g-B** (`:1806`) must carry the correlation step's attendee strings in — `packages/workflows/src/ports/meetingCloseout.ts` has **no `attendees` field** (grep: 0 hits). ⚠ **TWO SEPARATE MEETING PIPELINES EXIST AND ONLY THE OTHER ONE IS WIRED:** the production 7.6 chain (`buildActivities.ts:1076-1084` → `meetingOutputs.ts`) reads `attendees` as a rendered **string** for frontmatter/body and never touches `resolveEntity` — so "the meeting path" means two different things depending on which pipeline the reader has in mind, and only the dormant one has a refusal channel. ⇒ **13.8f-B is the KEYSTONE: it unblocks this consumer, and it belongs to worker, not knowledge.** ⛔ **The leg also has no committed scope** — no `13.8m-D` letter exists anywhere in this file and no brief beyond 214 (SOURCE consumer) and 226 (MEETING producer); it lives only as prose here, at `:68`, and as a Future-TODO in brief 226. Per 6(a5)(i) it is recorded as **unmappable rather than inferred into a brief.** ⚠ Corroborates `§ARM-RESEARCH` preconditions (2) and (3), which already recorded the same unwired-ness from the 13.8d side — **the facts were on the ledger; nothing had connected them to this leg's dispatchability.**
⛔ **RULE-7 CONSTRAINT THE FUTURE CONSUMER MUST BUILD FROM SCRATCH — 13.8m-B's sink shape CANNOT be reused.** `groundedPaths` (`meeting-rewrite.ts:112`) can contain literal human names: an attendee string flows `normalizeAttendees` → `EntityRef.name` → `entitySlug(raw)` (`match-keys.ts:23-31`, which lower-cases/hyphenates the RAW name) → `stubNotePathFor` (`entity-resolver.ts:146-155`) → `people/<person-name>.md`. **Nothing logs it today** (repo-wide `groundedPaths`: producer + 3 test files only, zero under `apps/`/`workflows/`), so **there is no existing redaction to inherit.** 13.8m-B's sink is `{workspaceId, codes: refusals}` — **reason CODES only** (`living-vault.ts:54,69`) — and `groundedPaths` is a **path array, not a code array**, so that shape does not transfer. ⇒ **any consumer must DERIVE a code or a count and never forward the array.** *(established 2026-07-30, orchestrator scoping pass)*

What is lost is that **a cross-workspace ATTEMPT occurred at all**, because that withhold renders identically to an ordinary no-match. ⇒ **an OBSERVABILITY GAP ON A SAFETY RULE, not a rule-4 breach.** ⛔ **Do NOT let this compress to "a WS-8 violation goes undetected"** — that reads as content crossing and is **false**; the precise and more useful claim is that a boundary **event** is unobservable, on the one rule where you would most want it logged. C's new branch is now the **only** place in the codebase that reads a `WithheldReason`, and it narrows to 2 of 7 members by type — so the other five, **including the WS-8 signal `ws_scope_mismatch`** (produced at `entity-resolver.ts`'s WS-8 defense-in-depth check), are **produced and reach zero consumers on BOTH paths**, indistinguishable from an ordinary no-match. Verified repo-wide: `WithheldReason`/`EntityResolution` appear in exactly TWO non-test files (⚠ **this said "three" in two places while enumerating two and asserting "nowhere else" — corrected 2026-07-30 by repo-wide re-grep; the third hit is a stale `packages/knowledge/dist/` build artifact, not a source file. The SUBSTANTIVE claim below is unaffected and was re-verified from source. This is item 8(b)'s de-inlined-literal defect in the very entry warning about unread signals: prefer the command `rg -l '\bWithheldReason\b' --include='*.ts'` over the value**), and `planner.ts`'s `collectEntities` never reads `res.reason` at all. ⛔ **Do not read 13.8m-B landing as "refusals now reach the operator"** — that claim is SOURCE-path-only until 13.8m-C lands (the 13.8k module-header scoping precedent). · **Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §6 (KN-7 "rejected AND audited" — the governing requirement; the error-convention § is deliberately not cited, it sits outside Phase 13's anchor set) · **Depends:** 13.8k · **Blocks:** 13.8f-B's ability to distinguish refused-from-nothing-to-do
**Files:** the synthesis receipt shape (`meeting-rewrite.ts` / `ingest-rewrite.ts`) · the worker consumer · tests
`admitGroundedPath` refuses correctly but the receipt has **no refusal channel**, so a poisoned-candidate attack produces a result byte-identical to a benign empty run — nothing is surfaced, nothing is audited, and 13.8f-B cannot tell "refused" from "nothing to do". That contradicts the repo's own KN-7 posture: rejected **and audited**, not merely rejected. Add a **code-only** `refusals: readonly GroundedPathRefusal[]` to the receipt (reason codes only — never paths or titles, rule 7) and have the worker consumer surface them. Deliberately NOT built in 13.8k because it is a receipt-shape change the worker consumes. **Done-when:** a refused path produces an observable, code-only refusal on the receipt; a benign empty run is distinguishable from a refused one; no path/title text crosses. *(origin: 2026-07-26 13.8k Step-9 Finding, knowledge)*

That prescription was mine, it was written before it was scoped, and scoping INVERTED it.** ⭐ **Established 2026-07-30, all reproducible:** (i) **`readonly` is NOT the house convention in `packages/contracts/src/models/` — it is 0 of ~30 files.** No Appendix-A seam-model interface declares a `readonly` field: `task.ts:22-35`, `proposed-action.ts:23-31`, `project.ts:28-46`, `shared-shapes.ts:26-29` all plain, and `entity-ref.ts:25-27` self-documents that it *"matches `proposed-action.ts` / `task.ts`."* ⇒ **`EntityRef` lacking `readonly` is the NORM; adding it would make `EntityRef` the 1-of-30 OUTLIER.** (ii) **The real split is `readonly`-by-category, not by accident:** Zod-as-source seam models (`src/models/*.ts`) → never `readonly`; ad hoc TS-only shapes → often (`primitives/result.ts:4-10`, `api/ui-safe.ts`). `EntityRef` is squarely the first category. (2) **contracts' `EntityRef` fields are NOT `readonly`; knowledge's ARE** (stated in `entity-ref.ts:7`) — so a naive swap **loosens immutability** at every knowledge consumer. Resolve deliberately (see 13.20, which can tighten it contracts-side).
**Done-when:** knowledge imports the type from `@sow/contracts`, the duplicate declaration is gone, the barrel re-export keeps `packages/evals` compiling untouched, model-supplied `entityRefs` are schema-validated at the `planSynthesis` boundary with a rejection that is **surfaced not silently dropped**, the existing `MAX_MODEL_ENTITY_REFS` fan-out cap (13.8h) still applies, and the immutability question is resolved rather than absorbed. *(origin: 2026-07-29, §DEC-CANDGATE sequencing contracts→knowledge→worker)*

### 13.21 — Re-establish `EntityRef` immutability at knowledge's own consumption sites (owner ruling: Option C)
- [x] DONE · `8b2a0af4` · 2026-07-30 · brief `docs/briefs/231-13.21-entityref-immutability-at-knowledge.md` (`@8472f385`) · **Depends:** 13.19 (`93cafe5f` — the swap that dropped the guarantee). ⭐ **Done-when verified against the COMMITTED TREE:** derived narrowing present at the choke point · **zero** separately-declared duplicate · the shallow-`Readonly` caveat recorded · the residual recorded in-code · the typecheck-not-runtime note on the pins · **`packages/contracts` untouched, owner ruling honoured** (2 files, both in `packages/knowledge`). `@sow/knowledge` 671 passed (+4); repo-wide typecheck 20/20; **`@sow/evals` run rather than reasoned about** — 613 passed, confirming the barrel-transitive consumer still compiles.
⛔ **THE DERIVED-CANNOT-DRIFT ARGUMENT HAS A LIMIT, and it was the orchestrator who asserted it away — found by code-quality review, not by design.** The brief claimed *"a future field add appears here automatically, still immutable"* and the Step-2.5 approval separately said *"`Readonly<>` being shallow is fine here — both fields are primitives."* **Those two statements are inconsistent.** ⇒ **`Readonly<T>` is SHALLOW: derived-cannot-drift is true about the field SET and says NOTHING about the DEPTH of the guarantee.** A future **non-primitive** field would appear automatically and would **not** be deeply immutable. Exact for today's two primitive fields; false as a general claim — and it was the load-bearing argument of the whole slice. The in-code comment now carries the caveat and says to re-examine if contracts adds a non-primitive field. ⚠ **The instructive part: both halves were the orchestrator's and neither was wrong alone** — a correct analysis of the present case was over-generalised to future cases in the same breath.
⭐ **THE RESIDUAL IS NARROWER THAN THE BRIEF CLAIMED, established by the implementer:** `entity-resolver.ts` is the **only** site in `packages/knowledge` importing the bare `EntityRef` **type** from `@sow/contracts` — `planner.ts` imports only the `EntityRefSchema` **value**. ⇒ a direct-from-contracts bypass is **hypothetical today with zero current instances**, not a live hole. Recorded in-code at the choke point and **deliberately not scanned for** (a negative claim over an unbounded import space is a detector, not a gate — 9.39's precedent). ⇒ **The owner's named cost — "C restores the guarantee per-consumer, so someone has to remember" — is narrowed to inheritance-for-the-intended-path, not eliminated.**
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §6 (KnowledgeWriter/GBrain/GCL — the synthesis consumers) · **Depends:** 13.19 · **Blocks:** — · **Brief:** `docs/briefs/231-13.21-entityref-immutability-at-knowledge.md` ⚠ *(a shared-contracts anchor was dropped from this line 2026-07-30: `spec-lint` correctly rejected it on the brief, because Option C modifies ZERO `packages/contracts` files, so citing that section would claim work this slice does not do. Fixed rather than waived.)*
⛔ **OWNER-RULED 2026-07-30, OPTION C of four.** Re-establish immutability **at knowledge's consumption sites**; ⛔ **contracts keeps its non-readonly convention untouched — do NOT add `readonly` to `EntityRef`, and do NOT open the ~30-model arc.** Rationale adopted from the orchestrator's recommendation, for its stated reason: **it is the only option that does not force one package's house convention onto another that deliberately chose the opposite.** ⚠ **This is KNOWLEDGE-area work. Contract stays on the 9.41 legs.**
⭐ **WHAT WAS ACTUALLY LOST, stated precisely because the existing code LOOKS like it already holds:** knowledge's consumption sites already say `readonly entityRefs?: readonly EntityRef[]` (`planner.ts:83`, `:348`; `attendee-refs.ts:54`, `:56`; `meeting-rewrite.ts:70`, `:80`). **`readonly T[]` prevents replacing an ELEMENT; it does nothing about `arr[0].name = "x"`.** 13.19 deleted knowledge's own `EntityRef` (which had `readonly name` / `readonly kind`) and re-exported contracts', whose fields are mutable — so **the element-level guarantee vanished while every array-level `readonly` stayed in place and kept the code looking protected.**
⭐ **THE LEVERAGE POINT IS A SINGLE LINE: `entity-resolver.ts:36`'s `export type { EntityKind, EntityRef };`.** Every knowledge consumer imports `EntityRef` from `./entity-resolver` (`planner.ts:46`, `attendee-refs.ts:35`), and `packages/evals` reaches it transitively through the `@sow/knowledge` barrel's `export *`. ⇒ **Re-exporting a DERIVED readonly form there gives every consumer the guarantee BY DEFAULT.**
⛔ **AND THE OBJECTION IT MUST DEFEAT — "isn't this re-adding the duplicate leg 2 deleted?" NO, and the distinction is load-bearing:** leg 2 deleted a **separately DECLARED** interface with its own field list, which could drift from contracts' version. A **derived** alias (`Readonly<EntityRef>` over the imported contracts type) **cannot drift, because it is computed from the single source of truth.** Declared duplication and derived narrowing are different things; only the first was the defect.
⭐ **ADDRESSES THE RULING'S OWN NAMED LIMIT.** The owner recorded that C restores the guarantee **per-consumer, not at the type**, so a future consumer *"has to remember"* — accepted cost, not oversight. **The choke-point re-export converts remembering into inheriting** for anyone importing through knowledge's intended path. ⚠ **Residual, and do NOT scanner it:** a consumer importing `EntityRef` **directly from `@sow/contracts`** inside knowledge still gets the mutable form. That is a negative claim over an unbounded import space — **a scanner there is a detector, not a gate (9.25's lesson, and 9.39 deleted one for exactly this reason).** Name it; do not police it.
**Done-when:** knowledge's consumers receive an element-immutable `EntityRef` through the existing import path with **no separately declared duplicate**; a test proves mutation of a ref obtained via knowledge's own import path **does not compile** (non-vacuity by the directive being genuinely required); `packages/evals` still compiles untouched (it only reads `ref.name` — verified); `EntityRefSchema.safeParse`'s output still assigns cleanly; and the direct-from-contracts residual is recorded in-code rather than scanned for. *(origin: 13.19's absorbed immutability clause; owner-ruled Option C 2026-07-30; numbered so it could be spec-linted and briefed)*

⭐ **NO RED-BY-MUTATION, AND THAT IS A DELIBERATE REVERSAL OF THIS BRIEF'S OWN ACCEPTANCE CRITERION — recorded because the reasoning outranks the slice.** The criterion said *"mutate one copy, watch it go RED, revert clean."* **The implementer refused it with evidence:** 13.20's author had already moved away from that pattern, since a transient mutation is a **real, visible break of a shared surface**, and **three implementers were writing concurrently.** ⇒ **Re-examining WHY the mutation was wanted dissolved the need for it:** the vacuity risk was never *"the comparison always passes"* but *"the comparison might be comparing one file to ITSELF"* — so **asserting path-distinctness + existence closes that mode structurally, permanently, and without breaking anything.** The mutation stopped being load-bearing, so its exposure bought nothing. **A better answer than either the brief's or the implementer's opening position, reached by pricing the objection instead of overruling it.**
⚠ **THE BOUND ON "STRUCTURALLY CLOSED", found by security review and recorded in-code:** the distinctness check is **lexical, not filesystem-identity** — a symlink aliasing the two paths would defeat it; a `realpath`/inode comparison would close it; **not done, because the condition is absent today.** ⛔ **The orchestrator had considered this and silently dropped it as over-engineering** — wrong not because the risk is real (different directories, no symlinks) but because **the ADD justified the design as closing the mode *structurally*, and that word was an over-claim while a known gap went unmentioned.** Review caught the gap the design's own justification had glossed.
⭐ **AND THE RECURSION CLOSES: 13.20 fixed a claimed-but-absent pin and shipped a claimed-but-unpinned equivalence; 13.22 pins the equivalence and shipped a claimed-but-unstated bound.** Each fix in this family has carried one unexamined claim forward. **The chain stops here only because the bound is now written down rather than judged unimportant.** ⇒ **`if the copies are ever deduplicated, DELETE this test — never retarget both reads at one path`** is recorded in-code, because that "fix" would restore a green check over an absent guarantee: the defect regenerating through its own remedy. Reviewers: security clean (confirming the string-equality is real, no swallowed exceptions or subset checks, and that skipping the mutation is sound **here** because this test is thin glue over well-tested primitives — unlike the custom parsing logic 13.20 correctly *did* mutation-test); code-quality 2 low, **both routed to the orchestrator rather than resolved unilaterally, because one concerned comment depth that came from the orchestrator's own review-cycle requirements** — an implementer silently trimming a required justification is how the reasoning disappears and what it prevented returns.
**Kind:** test · **Spec:** §2.5 (the import-direction root the scanners enforce) · **Depends:** 13.20 · **Blocks:** — · **Brief:** `docs/briefs/232-13.22-pure-root-scan-equivalence.md`
⚠ **WIDENS PHASE SCOPE** to §2.5, for the same reason and by the same rule 13.20 did (L92: active phase + declared widening); this is 13.20's residual and belongs beside it.
⭐ **THE RECURSION IS THE POINT, and it is why this is worth a slice rather than a shrug.** 13.20 existed because `packages/contracts/CLAUDE.md` **claimed a boundary test that did not exist** — an unbacked claim of mechanical coverage. **13.20's own residual is an unbacked claim of EQUIVALENCE between the two scanners it shipped.** Same defect class, one level in, produced by the fix for the first instance. **Recorded by 13.20's own reviewer as a Future TODO rather than discovered later** — which is why it is a small slice now instead of a surprise in a month.
**Verified 2026-07-30 (orchestrator):** `packages/contracts/test/_helpers/pure-root-scan.ts` and `packages/domain/test/_helpers/pure-root-scan.ts` are **byte-identical today** (`diff -q` clean, 105 lines each) and export the same five symbols — `PureRootFile`, `PureRootViolationReason`, `PureRootViolation`, `PureRootScanResult`, `scanPureRootViolations`. ⭐ **All package-specific configuration is passed in as ARGUMENTS by each package's `pure-root.test.ts`, so the duplicated code is pure logic with NO legitimate reason to diverge** — which is exactly what makes a lockstep assertion the right shape rather than an over-constraint.
⛔ **THE FAILURE MODE, stated concretely because "duplication" undersells it:** someone fixes a scanner bug in one copy and not the other. **`packages/domain`'s boundary test then keeps passing against a STALE, WEAKER scanner** — green, and no longer checking what its sibling checks. **A divergence makes the guarantee quietly smaller without ever failing**, which is this round's signature defect and the reason `readonly T[]` hid a missing element-level guarantee two tasks ago.
**Done-when:** a divergence between the two copies **fails a test**, proven non-vacuous by mutating one copy and watching it go RED; the fix adds no cross-package coupling that the §2.5 import-direction invariant would have to except; and if the two are instead **deduplicated**, the shared location does not put a test helper into `packages/contracts`' public surface or make `packages/domain`'s suite depend on `packages/contracts`' test internals. *(origin: 13.20's reviewer-deferred Future TODO, `0a6d6629`; promoted + numbered 2026-07-30 on the lead's approval, per 6(a5) — a residual is not a specification)*

## Phase 14 — Onboarding, Config Surfaces & Runtime Substrate
**Goal:** Give a real user a way to exist in the system, give the packaged app the local processes it needs to run, and stand up the two registries the spine resolves against (typed-Project registry; cross-workspace link store) — replacing `provisionDev` fixtures with real onboarding. Posture: pure-build / substrate — crosses no arming hard line (loopback-only; no external network / spend / credential). · **Spec anchors:** §19.1 (primary) · §11 · §13 · §5 · §8 · §6 · §4 · §16 · **Track:** desktop, worker · **Depends on (phases):** 3, 8, 9, 11
**Gate:** ✅ /phase-exit 14 CLEAR (2026-07-15) — checklist: docs/archive/IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md Part 3 · audits: docs/audits/14-reachability-desktop.md, 14-reachability-worker.md, 14-arch-drift.md

### 14.1 — Workspace onboarding & provisioning (real replacement for provisionDev)
- [x] DONE · `e1e4cfd` · 2026-07-15 · closes G58
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §19.1 · §11 · §5 (WS-8 registry) · **Depends:** P9 (§11 UI), P8 (§10 API), P3 (§5 WS-8 registry) · **Blocks:** 14.2, 14.5, 14.6, 14.7
**Files:** apps/desktop/renderer/surfaces/onboarding/, renderer/store/scope.ts, apps/worker/src/api/procedures/onboarding.ts, apps/worker/src/composition/provisionDev.ts (`provisionWorkspace`)
Real onboarding (create workspace → choose vault root → pick Simple/Professional/Founder/Advanced preset) mints workspaces and unions each id into the fail-closed WS-8 registry as the production provisioning path: `provisionWorkspace` builds a safe-default `Workspace` → upserts into the dual-dialect `WorkspaceConfigRepository` → unions into the WS-8 `{workspaceIds}` registry, **upsert-precedes-union** so a partial failure never yields a registry-known-but-config-less workspace. Isolation-class immutability guard: re-onboarding an existing id with a changed `type` → `workspace_type_immutable` (closes a rule-4/5 latent downgrade); same-type stays idempotent. vaultRoot maps to the existing `markdownRepoPath` — no frozen-`Workspace`-seam change; `preset` deferred to 14.5. Worker leg `e1e4cfd`; desktop onboarding UI + preset picker `ad624a16`. A workspace absent from the registry still gets zero read path (fail-closed).

### 14.2 — Connector configuration surface + per-workspace connector-instance registry
- [x] DONE · `6f639a6` · 2026-07-15 · closes G59
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §19.1 · §11 · §8 · **Depends:** 14.1, P6 (§8 gateways), P2 (§4 storage) · **Blocks:** Phase-16 composition, Phase-23 arming
**Files:** apps/desktop/renderer/surfaces/connectors/, packages/db connector-instance table + migration, apps/worker/src/api/procedures/connectorConfig.ts
A connectors/settings surface lets a user add a connector, bind it to a workspace, record its tokenRef, enable/pause it, and set cadence — each choice persisted as a per-vendor+workspace connector-instance record (the record Phase-16 composition + Phase-23 arming consume). tokenRef is **reference-only** (rule 7, structural whitelisting parser — no live call); register defaults **paused** (fail-safe); WS-8 membership-gated (registry-read-fault fails closed); `connector_instance_workspace_immutable` binding guard. NO real credential is provisioned here — binding a real credential/transport is Phase 17/23. Dual-dialect `connector_instance` table + `ConnectorInstanceRepository` db `bda2c91` + config path `6f639a6`; desktop connectors surface `7d141528`.

## Phase 19 — gbrain Write-Back, Parity, Provenance, Embedding-Egress & Cost Ledger

**Goal:** App owns vault→gbrain population and vault↔gbrain parity — post-commit incremental sync + durable outbox, HMAC provenance stamping on every KW commit, reconcile pass + real read/write gbrain clients, the rebuild-from-Markdown oracle, the serving-coverage 4-way gate, plus the two under-marked hard-lines (employer-embedding egress at the gbrain layer + a durable cross-run cost ledger upgrading the Phase-18 budget-gate port). Posture: HARD-LINE/mixed (crossing 3) — engines build dormant against fake clients; binding the real gbrain write/read clients + the real HMAC signing key + the employer-embedding backend is the crossing, owner confirm per crossing. · **Spec anchors:** §19.6 (primary); §6 write-through/divergence/parity + serving-coverage reader + reconcile trigger + provenance stamping; §4 operational stores (`GbrainSyncOutboxStore`, `ParityReportStore`, cost ledger); §5 embedding-egress veto; §7 budget gate; §16 redaction; Appendix A (ParityReport, SignedProvenanceStamp, GbrainReadGrant, FactProvenance). · **Track:** knowledge · worker · **Depends on (phases):** 18 (real KW-commit content + budget-gate port), 17 (Keychain HMAC signing key + `getSecret`), 14 (app-managed `gbrain serve` substrate)
**Gate:** — (no /phase-exit run)

Every real-client leg is byte-equivalent to the shipped dormant default when the clients, HMAC key, and embedding backend are unbound (the owner-gated binds are the only behavior change); each `=== true` arming knob carries a truthy-not-`true` guard.

### 24.6 — Safety-assertion audit: every claim DERIVED from the state that governs it, at the scope it applies to (⛔ RUN BEFORE GO-LIVE)
- [ ] OPEN — ⛔ **owner-approved 2026-07-26; required BEFORE go-live** (placed here, not on the Phase-9 exit — see the scoping note)
**Kind:** audit · **Spec:** §5 (egress/consent posture), §11 (surfaces), §16, safety rules 5/7 · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** go-live
**Files:** audit output → `docs/audits/` · scope spans ALL areas + `docs/design/**` + `docs/**`
**Why:** this round produced FOUR rule-5 issues sharing one shape — a safety claim ASSUMED rather than derived (the hardcoded chrome "Egress: local-only" pill · the 9.10-A pre-store constant · the provisioning re-seed overwriting a revoked ack · the `zeroEgressOnly` contract drift) — plus a FIFTH with the inverse construction (the Copilot notice's absence implying safety). Each was found by building or reviewing, never by planning; contracts L56/L64 are the banked rules.
**⭐ HEADLINE FINDING THIS AUDIT EXISTS FOR — a TESTED false assurance (2026-07-26, worker 9.22).** An untested false assurance is a gap. A **tested** one is an ACTIVE DEFENSE of the defect: `egressCommands.test.ts:83` and `:178` both asserted `zeroEgressOnly === true` after a revoke, pinning a claim that was false whenever the workspace allowed a cloud processor. The suite therefore made the CORRECT fix look like a regression — the next person to attempt it sees red and backs out, **and backs out with the confidence a failing test confers.** That inverts the review gate from a check into a lock. ⚠ **Consequence for this audit's scope: an audit that reads only production code will not find this class at all — it lives in the ASSERTIONS.** Corollary observed in the same slice: after fixing a construction, re-sweep the TESTS for assertions encoding the old behaviour; worker reported one pinning assertion, the lead found a second (`:83`, a full-object `toEqual` — the more brittle form), and an orchestrator sweep found two further stale FAKES modelling a now-impossible state. One report, four artifacts (contracts L61/L69).

**⛔ FIVE BINDING CONSTRAINTS (owner-set; do not soften any of them):**
⚠ **The count said FOUR while listing FIVE, from 2026-07-26 until 2026-07-31 — corrected by the lead, and the reasoning matters more than the fix.** Constraint 5 is stamped *"added 2026-07-26"*, the same day this task was owner-approved, so it was almost certainly appended during the same assembly and the header simply never caught up. ⛔ **But that is INFERRED, not verified — nobody has confirmed the owner authored constraint 5, and this edit does not claim they did.** ⭐ **The count was fixed anyway, because a wrong count is unsafe under EITHER provenance:** on a list whose instruction is *"do not soften any of them,"* a reader who counts five under a header saying four cannot tell **which** one is surplus — so the error puts all five in doubt, including the four that are unambiguously the owner's. **Fixing the count protects constraints 1–4 regardless of who wrote the fifth.** ⭐ **And note what this edit is NOT:** it changes a numeral, not a requirement — nobody's obligations move by one character. That is the line between repairing owner-set text and amending it, and the amendment case (`(a0)(ix)(1)`) stays with the owner precisely because it *would* move an obligation. **Provenance of constraint 5 is an open question for the owner, recorded in handoff 020 — do not let this note be read as ratification.** *(L94 again: the addition landed in the list and never in the count.)*
1. **CONCEPT-LEVEL enumeration, never known string forms.** The orchestrator's own design-doc sweep needed THREE iterations because the first grepped the phrasings already known — 4 of 9 sites found. **A string-shaped audit returns a confident false all-clear, which is worse than not running it** (contracts L64). Report the search KEY and the dry-iteration count.
2. **BOTH mechanisms.** Asserted-constant (a claim stated from a constant/default-seed) AND **fail-open-by-omission** (a claim implied by a MISSING element — e.g. a warning that renders only when data is present, so "no data" and "nothing to warn about" look identical). They look nothing alike in code and a sweep tuned to one will miss the other.
3. **DOCS AND DESIGN AUTHORITY IN SCOPE, not just code.** The chrome pill's mandate lived in a locked screen-GENERATION prompt and in a normative governance principle — **a code-only audit would have cleared it** while the generator kept reproducing the defect.
4. **The question is "is this claim DERIVED from the state that governs it, at the SCOPE that state applies to" — NOT "is this claim currently true."**
5. **TEST ASSERTIONS AND FAKES ARE IN SCOPE** (added 2026-07-26 from the headline finding above). A safety claim can be defended by a test that pins it, or normalized by a fake that models it — neither appears in production code. Sweep assertions and fixtures with the same concept-level method, and treat a test asserting a safety value as a claim requiring the same derivation check as the code. A claim that happens to be true today but cannot move when the governing state moves is a decoration that reads as a guarantee.
**Scoping note (orchestrator's call, 2026-07-26):** deliberately NOT ridden on the Phase-9 exit. Its scope is wider than any single phase (both mechanisms · all areas · docs + design authority, which is not Phase-9 territory), and the Phase-9 gate is already blocked on 9.21 plus five `[~]` legs — hanging a cross-cutting pre-go-live audit off it would either delay the gate or get the audit rushed to unblock it. Phase 24 is where hardening lives and is pre-go-live by construction.

### 24.7 — Every interactive-Copilot policy denial builds an `AuditSignal` and DISCARDS it (24.6 finding F3 — rules 4/5/6)
- [x] DONE `0c8de4b2` 2026-08-11 (worker) — **arming-block condition (d) remedy, orchestrator-verified against the committed diff** (both live deny branches confirmed calling `persistDenial`; the redaction-safety gate, the audit-mislabeling fix, and the `briefing` deps gap all read directly from source, not taken on Step-9 report alone) · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** the arming crossings' observability
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §5 (egress/consent posture), §16, safety rules 4/5/6
**Files:** `apps/worker/src/api/procedures/copilot.ts:191-195` · `copilotAgentSynthesis.ts:383-388` · `packages/policy/src/audit-signal.ts:137-141` · `packages/policy/src/visibility.ts` (identical shape) · the tRPC error path in `apps/worker/src/api/server.ts` (no `onError` exists)
⛔ **THE FINDING:** every §5 egress-veto and ING-7 admission denial on the **interactive Copilot path** constructs a redaction-safe `AuditSignal` via `buildAuditSignal` — **whose entire purpose is to make the denial auditable** — and every caller discards it, threading only `decision.reason` into the error `cause.code`, never `decision.audit`. **`toAuditRecordInput`, the only function that turns an `AuditSignal` into a persistable `AuditRecord`, has exactly ONE repo-wide match: its own definition. Zero callers.** There is no tRPC error middleware and **no `HealthItem` is minted for these denial classes on this path** (`security_violation`/`policy_denial`/`egress_denied` are emitted only from the batch `sourceIngestion.ts` workflow). `visibility.ts` shares the identical `buildAuditSignal`→nowhere shape, which puts **rule 4** in the same position.
⭐ **SEVERITY, PRECISELY — THE GUARANTEE HOLDS; ONLY DETECTION IS LOST.** Every denial still returns `err`, the action is still blocked, nothing crosses the boundary. ⛔ **What is unobservable is that a policy boundary was PROBED AT ALL:** one accidental trip and a sustained adversarial campaign against the employer-egress veto or the untrusted-content admission gate are **byte-identical at every durable, queryable surface.** ⚠ **Do NOT compress this to "a rule-5 violation goes undetected"** — that reads as content crossing and is **false**. Same distinction the `WithheldReason` entry in Carry-forward 2 insists on.
⚠ **Broader than the tracked `#45`** (which covers the §5 veto's dropped signal): this is the **interactive** path across **three** safety rules. **Verified by the auditor's refutation attempt:** searched for any downstream reader of `.audit` in `apps/worker/src` (only unrelated `backends.repos.audit`, the operational-audit repo for KW commits — a different type), and for tRPC-level logging (none exists).
**Done-when:** an egress-veto or admission denial on the interactive path lands a durable, queryable record (code-only per rule 7 — never raw content, never the prompt), the benign path mints **zero** records (so the distinction is real in both directions — L86), and a sustained-probing pattern is distinguishable from a quiet workspace. *(origin: 2026-07-31 24.6 audit partition FO-1; `docs/audits/001-…`)*
⛔ **FINDING, not just a scoping note (lead's own framing) — the task's own `Files:` line above is WRONG about the tRPC error path.** `apps/worker/src/api/server.ts`'s "no `onError` exists" was written as if adding one were the fix. **It is structurally incapable of ever being the fix**: this codebase's tRPC procedures follow §16 (never throw, always return `Result` as data), so a policy denial travels as successful transport data, never a thrown `TRPCError` — an `onError` hook fires only on unexpected throws and **would never see a policy-denial `err` under any implementation.** This is `(a0)(viii)` face 2 — the tracker being wrong about WHAT, in a way that would have produced a defect (a slice spent wiring a hook that cannot fire) if followed literally. Established by scoping research (`docs/briefs/252-…`) before dispatch, not discovered mid-slice. **The real fix sites are the two live choke points identified in that brief: `runGovernedCopilotSynthesis` (egress veto, unconditional) and `createAgentRuntimeCopilotSynthesis`'s `synthesize` closure (ING-7 admission, agentic-mode only).**

### 24.12 — The `LegacyContentPolicy` `assign` bridge is a hardcoded rule-4 decision whose soundness precondition is a COMMENT, and it is LIVE (24.6 round-2 finding AC-2b/F9 — ⚠ SAFETY rule 4)

⛔ **WHAT IT IS: the precondition that makes `assign` sound is a COMMENT enforced by human discipline, with no gate and no detector.** The in-code operator guard reads *"do NOT save non-personal-business content UNPREFIXED (the {assign} bridge is sound only while the brain holds a single workspace's unprefixed content)."* ⭐ **The obvious enforcement point was traced and is ABSENT: `knowledge-writer/writer.ts` does not enforce workspace-prefixed note paths — `workspaceId` rides the plan's metadata and audit trail, never the PATH.** ⇒ nothing structurally prevents a foreign-workspace note landing unprefixed, after which it is attributed **`legacy`** and **kept for personal-business asks** — silently: no error, no drop, and **no audit signal distinguishing it from a genuine personal-business legacy note.**
⭐ **THIS IS CONTRACTS L123 — *"MUST is prose, not a gate"* — ON SAFETY RULE 4, ARMED.** The comment's own claim (*"INERT today — only personal-business has content"*) is the asserted-constant shape 24.6 exists to find: **true today, structurally unable to move when the state moves.**

⇒ **ACTION (recommendation, NOT a ruling — owner-gated):** write-time structural enforcement so a foreign-workspace note **cannot** land unprefixed; failing that, a detector that fires the moment non-personal-business content appears unprefixed. ⛔ **An operator guard in a comment is precisely what 24.6 was chartered to stop accepting.** ⚠ **Follow-up AC-2b explicitly did NOT do: check whether any periodic scan/monitor elsewhere would detect foreign unprefixed content** — that would mitigate, not resolve. **Do not treat this as actioned until that check is run.**

- [x] DONE `7a4fe0ac` 2026-08-11 (providers-integrations) — ⚠ **tick + `--snapshot` both missed at Step-10 time, caught late by the lead; repaired here** · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** any wiring of `reconcileVault` / the fs-watch out-of-band reconciler

⭐ **REMEDY BUILT — Path B (fail-fast boot guard), not Path A.** `assertReconcileVaultBootSafe(verify?: KwSigVerifier): void` in `reconcile.ts`, throws iff the resolved verifier is the `defaultVerifyKwSig` placeholder singleton (reference-equality); pinned by 4 tests incl. two `execSync` repo-wide grep censuses (mirroring `neutralizer-single-source.test.ts`) making "zero production callers" and "no production override" standing CI trip-wires, not one-off checks. Path A deferred as correctly out of scope — `reconcileVault` has zero production callers today, so a real HMAC verifier has no live consumer to validate against yet; **the class fix is L106-shaped** (build the guard now, the real verifier when a real wiring task exists). ⚠ **Worker/db territory, not knowledge-only** — the defect is precisely that the knowledge surface's *"single path"* claim is not what is wired.
⛔⛔ **DESCRIPTION CORRECTED 2026-08-11 FROM SOURCE BY worker-implementer — *"COLLAPSE TO ONE IMPLEMENTATION"* WAS A RECORDED-AND-WRONG DESCRIPTION OF THIS FIX, and it is the kind a future reader IMPLEMENTS.** **Measured:** `@sow/policy`'s `ApprovedLink` is consumed **only** by `denyDirectCrossWorkspaceRaw`/`guardCrossWorkspaceRawRead` — **the direct-raw-retrieval hard-denial gate, a DIFFERENT mechanism this path never exercises** — while `@sow/db`'s `CrossWorkspaceLinkRow` is the durable record this path already uses. ⇒ **they are not two representations of one concept, so there is nothing to reconcile and nothing to collapse.** ⭐⭐ **THE CORRECT STATEMENT OF THE DEFECT: the link's frozen `scopeVisibilityLevel` check is a LEGITIMATE, DISTINCT GATE — the owner's SPECIFIC PER-LINK GRANT — and it SHOULD stay frozen, precisely so an owner's granted scope cannot silently WIDEN. What is missing is a SECOND, INDEPENDENT gate: the source workspace's LIVE ceiling.** ⇒ ***TWO GATES THAT MUST BOTH PASS, NOT ONE REPLACING THE OTHER.*** ⛔ **Do not "fix" this by removing or relaxing the per-link check — that would trade an owner grant for a workspace default and is the opposite of the fix.** ⭐ **AND THE SKIPS MUST NOT BE SILENT (orchestrator ADD, lead-backed): a `visibility_exceeds_source` on a STORED row is AMBIGUOUS between a tightened ceiling (benign) and a TAMPERED ROW — which is the case `serveProjection` EXISTS to catch.** ⇒ **a silent `continue` does not merely lose a signal, it DISARMS THE SPECIFIC MECHANISM THE FIX ROUTES THROUGH** — *guarantee holds, detection lost*, the sibling defect minted while fixing the parent. **Rule 7: a count or closed code, never a path.** **Done-when:** ONE of — (i) the per-row check ALSO routes through `serveProjection`/`validateProjectionVisibility` **against the CURRENT source workspace**, as a second gate alongside the per-link grant; or (ii) the two implementations are deliberately kept and the link's scope bounds are **re-derived against the live workspace default at every read**, with the reason two independent approval representations exist recorded as a decision rather than left as drift. ⛔ **Either way the four prose comments must stop asserting a path that is not called** — a comment describing an unimplemented architecture is this audit's own mechanism. *(origin: 2026-08-11, 24.6 round 3, partition WS-1; three legs re-verified by the orchestrator)*

### 24.18 — `GclProjection.visibilityLevel` is self-declared by the producer; the gate enforces a CEILING, never a derivation (24.6 round-3 finding WS-1/F14 — rule 4)
- [x] DONE `91a68725` 2026-08-11 (knowledge) · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** any real `ProjectionSource` implementation
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §5, §6 · **Safety rule 4**
**Files:** `packages/contracts/src/models/gcl-projection.ts:62-70` · `packages/policy/src/visibility.ts:143-149` (`isWithinDefault`) · `packages/workflows/src/activities/buildGclProjection.ts:46-52`
**THE FINDING:** `admitProjection` → `validateProjectionVisibility` checks exactly one thing — `visibilityRank(declared) <= visibilityRank(workspace.defaultVisibility)`. ⭐ **A CEILING, not a floor and not a derivation.** A producer may declare `visibilityLevel: "full"` for a `projectionType` that should be `isolated`, and the gate admits it as long as the workspace ceiling permits. **The raw-content-shape check is orthogonal — it bounds the VALUES, not whether the declared LEVEL matches the content.**
⛔ **WHAT MOVES THE STATE WITHOUT MOVING THE CLAIM: raising `Workspace.defaultVisibility`** (available to any workspace owner / the onboarding flow) **silently widens what every ALREADY-STORED self-declared `visibilityLevel` is now permitted to cross with** — the claim *"this projection is `full`"* is never re-examined against what the data actually is.
⚠ **Graded MEDIUM, not HIGH, and the reason is worth keeping:** the gap is **self-disclosed in-code** as an `arch_gap` (*"projectionType taxonomy unspecified upstream"*), so it is **not a false assurance of coverage** (contracts L96's distinction), and every `ProjectionSource`/`ProjectionGate` is an interface with **zero concrete non-test implementations.**
**Done-when:** the eventual `projectionType` taxonomy (§5/§6) supplies a **derivation** — `projectionType` ⇒ permitted `visibilityLevel` — not merely a ceiling, and it lands **before** any real `ProjectionSource` ships. *(origin: 2026-08-11, 24.6 round 3, WS-1)*

### 24.19 — The "key-name-independent, recursive" raw-content gate does not traverse every container shape it claims to cover (24.6 round-3 finding WS-1/F15 — rule 4)
- [x] DONE `4cef394f` 2026-08-11 (knowledge) · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** —
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §5, §6 · **Safety rule 4** · **Files:** `packages/contracts/src/models/gcl-projection.ts:37-51` (`isRawContentShaped`/`carriesRawContent`) · `packages/contracts/schemas/gcl-projection.schema.json:21-24` · `packages/contracts/test/models/gcl-projection.test.ts`
**THE CLAIM (in-code, `:23-26`):** *"the gate is KEY-NAME-INDEPENDENT: it scans EVERY value recursively (nested objects + arrays) and rejects any string that is multi-line OR exceeds the summary length cap, regardless of key name."*
⛔ **FALSE FOR TWO CONTAINER SHAPES.** The traversal is `Array.isArray` → recurse, else `typeof === "object"` → recurse over **`Object.entries(value)`**, which enumerates only **own enumerable string-keyed** properties. Both of these are `typeof === "object"` yet yield `[]`, so the function falls through to *"not raw-content-shaped"* **without inspecting their contents**: (1) **`Map`/`Set`** — `Object.entries(new Map([["x","…multi-line raw…"]]))` → `[]`; (2) **non-enumerable own properties** via `Object.defineProperty(…, {enumerable:false})`.
⛔ **AND THERE IS NO AJV BACKSTOP:** the generated schema has `sanitizedPayload.additionalProperties: {}` — **unconstrained** — so **the Zod `.refine` is the ONLY enforcement, not defence-in-depth over a schema floor.** (Read from the emitted file, not inferred.)
⚠ **Nested arrays, objects-in-arrays and deep nesting ARE handled correctly** — confirmed against the module's own adversarial tests, **which stop exactly there.** `Array.prototype.some`'s short-circuit is correct, not a bypass.
⭐ **No end-to-end leak found in currently-wired code** (no producer builds such a payload; the one concrete `sanitizedPayload` consumer filters to `string|number|boolean` and would drop a `Map` — **fail-safe by accident, not by design**). ⛔ **The finding is that the code's own STATED GUARANTEE is false for these shapes and nothing tests the boundary.**
**Done-when:** either `sanitizedPayload`'s runtime shape is structurally restricted to what `Object.entries`/`JSON.parse` can produce (a non-plain-object/array value is its own rejection reason — cheap and structural, the preferred fix), **or** the traversal iterates `Reflect.ownKeys` plus `Map`/`Set`; **and adversarial tests exist for both shapes.** ⚠ **Carried-forward unknown, do not assume it away:** whether the concrete `GclProjectionRepository` adapters silently drop a `Map` on JSON serialization was **not verified.** *(origin: 2026-08-11, 24.6 round 3, WS-1)*
