### 24.21 — `buildToolWriteHealthSignal`'s `kind` union conflates a HOLD with an ERRORED ATTEMPT (origin: 24.16 investigation; §16)
- [ ] OPEN · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** any honest re-pointing of the tool-write health class
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §16 (error convention), §8 · **Files:** `packages/integrations/src/health/health-signal.ts:118-135` · `packages/integrations/src/tools/outbox.ts:144` · `packages/integrations/test/health-signal.test.ts`
**THE FINDING:** `buildToolWriteHealthSignal` takes `kind: "write_through_failed" | "schema_rejection"`, and its own doc comment says the first covers ***"blocked drain / target unreachable."*** ⛔ **Those are two different conditions — a PRECONDITION HOLD (never attempted) and an ATTEMPT THAT ERRORED — and that is precisely the distinction task 13.15 added `write_through_blocked` to the frozen `FailureClass` enum to preserve.** ⇒ **the conflation is upstream of the class constant, so no choice of constant can express both callers correctly.**
⭐ **WHY IT SURFACED ONLY NOW, and it is the useful part: while neither enum member existed, the conflation was UNEXPRESSIBLE and therefore invisible. 13.15 shipped the vocabulary to distinguish them, which turned a limitation into a DEFECT without anyone editing this file** — the same shape as `### 24.16`'s citation rot, one level up: **the world moved and the code's expressiveness did not.**
⚠ **DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED IN 24.16** — splitting the union is a **behaviour change with a real severity decision attached** (`outbox_blocked` defaults to `error`, `write_through_blocked` to `warn`), and 24.16 was scoped as a citation-rot fix. ⛔ **Do not let this be settled by whoever next edits the constant.**
⚠ **Currently INERT and that is the wrong reason to close it:** the only production path into this function is `outboxHealth`, which has **zero callers** (`### 24.8`). ⇒ **fix the taxonomy BEFORE 24.8 wires the signal, not after** — once wired, the same change becomes a live operator-facing severity change.
**Done-when:** the `kind` union distinguishes a hold from an errored attempt, each mapping to the frozen member that matches it; the severity difference is a stated decision rather than a side effect; and a test pins **both** directions. *(origin: 2026-08-11, 24.16 investigation, providers-integrations)*

### 24.22 — `routingHints.trustLevel` is DEAD ON ARRIVAL repo-wide: every source-built AgentJob hardcodes `untrusted` (origin: 24.14 investigation; safety rule 6)
- [ ] OPEN · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** any downstream consumer of a capture's trust classification (§13.6 trigger work, §13.8 confined-auto tier)
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §8, §13.6, §13.8 · **Safety rule 6** (ING-7) · **Files:** `apps/worker/src/composition/source-extraction.ts:173` · `packages/workflows/src/activities/runAgentJob.ts:152` · **worker territory**
**THE FINDING (providers-integrations, while establishing `### 24.14`'s premises — correctly FLAGGED, not built cross-area):** `source-extraction.ts:173` hardcodes `trustLevel: "untrusted"` **unconditionally for every source** (its comment: *"ING-7 / inv-2: an imported source is untrusted raw content — always"*), `runAgentJob.ts:152` does the same for meeting transcripts, and **repo-wide there are ZERO reads of `routingHints.trustLevel`** across `packages/workflows/src`, `packages/knowledge/src` and `apps/worker/src`.
⭐ **THE FRAMING THAT MATTERS, lifted verbatim from the reporter because it is the whole point: `24.14`'s fix must not silently become *"safe for the WRONG reason (nobody reads it)"* instead of *"safe for the RIGHT reason (nothing forgeable grants it)."*** ⇒ **the two are indistinguishable from the outside today, and only one survives the §13.6 trigger work.**
⚠ **THE HARDCODED VALUE IS FAIL-SAFE AND IS NOT ITSELF THE DEFECT** — *always untrusted* is the correct ING-7 posture for imported content. ⛔ **The defect is that a produced trust CLASSIFICATION has no consumer, so the whole `routingHints.trustLevel` channel is a produced-and-dropped signal** (the L106 class, five instances) — **and a future slice wiring trust propagation would find a field that has never once been read, with no test pinning what reading it should mean.**
**Done-when:** either `routingHints.trustLevel` is read where trust is decided (with the fail-safe `untrusted` default preserved for imported sources), **or** the field is removed and the honest posture stated in one place — ⛔ **and whichever is chosen, the §13.6 trigger slice cannot silently inherit a channel nobody has ever consumed.** *(origin: 2026-08-11, 24.14 Step-1 investigation, providers-integrations)*

### 24.23 — `mapWriteFailure`'s `default:` SILENTLY ABSORBS a new closed-union member, flattening `workspace_path_violation` to `commit_failed` (origin: 24.12; §16)
- [ ] OPEN · **Depends:** 24.12 (which introduces the new code) · **Blocks:** honest reporting of a 24.12 rejection at the workflows boundary
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §16 (error convention) · **Files:** `packages/workflows/src/ports/meetingCloseout.ts:424-429` (`KnowledgeCommitFailureCode`, a closed 5-member union) · `packages/workflows/src/activities/commitKnowledge.ts` (`mapWriteFailure`) · `apps/worker/test/.../proof-spine-composition.test.ts` — **worker territory**
**THE FINDING (knowledge-implementer, traced to ROOT rather than observed, while chasing a failing test its own slice caused):** `24.12` adds a `workspace_path_violation` write-failure code. **`mapWriteFailure`'s switch has no case for it and falls to `default: return "commit_failed"`** ⇒ **a workspace-scope rejection is reported as a generic commit failure.**
⭐⭐ **THE GENERALISATION IS WORTH MORE THAN THE INSTANCE: a `default:` BRANCH IN A MAPPER OVER A CLOSED UNION IS A SILENT ABSORBER FOR EVERY FUTURE MEMBER.** The new code arrives, **nothing fails**, and it quietly means something else. ⛔ **And this repo already knows the answer: `defaultSeverityForFailureClass` over the sibling `FailureClass` union is `assertNever`-GUARDED, so adding a member there is a COMPILE ERROR.** ⇒ ⭐ **two mappers over closed unions in one repo — one exhaustive, one absorbing.** ⚠ **THIRD instance this round of *the vocabulary grew and the consumer did not* (`24.16` citation rot · `24.21` kind conflation · this).** Banked as contracts **L134**.
⛔ **SEVERITY, PRECISELY — the compressed version is FALSE: the WRITE IS STILL REJECTED. Fail-closed holds; only the REASON is lost.** ⭐ *Guarantee holds, detection degraded.* **Do NOT write *"the gate does not work across the boundary."***
⚠ **The sibling test-fixture fix was taken by knowledge under contracts L121's narrow form** (its own change broke it, assertion-preserving, one line, own commit) — ⛔ **this enum widening was NOT: a design change to a closed union in another track's port is a crossing, and it was correctly routed rather than reached.**
**Done-when:** `KnowledgeCommitFailureCode` carries a member for the workspace-scope rejection **and** `mapWriteFailure` is **exhaustiveness-guarded (`assertNever`) rather than `default:`-terminated**, so the NEXT member added is a compile error rather than a silent re-classification. ⛔ **Adding only the member and leaving the `default:` re-arms the same trap for the member after this one.** *(origin: 2026-08-11, 24.12 reviewer chase, knowledge-implementer)*

### 24.24 — `16.4`'s "fail-VISIBLE coverage-degrade" signal is DISCARDED one hop past where it is minted (24.6 round-4 finding ACC-1/F20)
- [ ] OPEN · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** any claim that Drive partial-coverage is operator-visible
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §8, §16 · **Files:** `packages/workflows/src/activities/connectorPoll.ts:61-80` (`projectSyncResult`) · `packages/workflows/src/workflows/connectorSyncHealth.ts:451-461` · *(producer, correct as-is)* `packages/integrations/src/connectors/gateway.ts:198-217` — **worker territory**
⛔ **THE FINDING, orchestrator-verified at the decisive hop:** `16.4`'s task text asserts *"Drive `incompleteSearch=true` now mints a fail-VISIBLE coverage-degrade `GatewayHealthSignal` (records still commit) **instead of being silently dropped**."* **The gateway does mint it — on the `"advanced"` branch, correctly.** ⛔ **Then `projectSyncResult` reads `if (result.status === "advanced") { return base; }` and `base` carries NO `healthSignal`/`healthReason` field at all** — that field is attached only in the `else` (held/degraded) branch. ⇒ **the coverage-degrade signal, which BY CONSTRUCTION lives only on the `"advanced"` branch, is discarded unconditionally.** `connectorSyncHealth.ts` mirrors the same split, so `deps.health.surface(...)` is never reached for this case.
⭐ **NEVER TRUE — the drop was introduced by the SAME task (`16.4`, `07e27aea`) that claims the opposite.** It did not regress later. ⚠ Its precondition `14.3` had honestly disclaimed the producer as *"unbuilt… route when built — not a gap"*; **`16.4` built the producer and never wired the two downstream hops that would make *route when built* true, and the disclaimer was never revisited.**
⭐⭐ **THE TEST EVIDENCE IS THE SHARPEST PART: `connector-sync-health.test.ts:376-387`'s own `projectSyncResult` unit test constructs an `"advanced"` input WITHOUT a `healthSignal` and asserts `healthReason` is `undefined` — so it NEVER EXERCISES THE 16.4 SCENARIO (`"advanced"` WITH a signal present).** ⇒ **the drop is UNTESTED, not merely undetected** — a suite that pins the branch beside the defect without ever entering it.
⛔ **SEVERITY, PRECISELY: *guarantee holds, DETECTION is lost.* Drive records still commit correctly and no safety invariant is violated** — what is lost is the operator's ability to know a sync page was PARTIAL. ⚠ **Dormant today (§ARM-23 unbound) — and ⛔ ARMING PHASE 23 WILL NOT FIX IT: the drop is structural and independent of arming state.**
⭐ **AND THE PHRASING NOTE THAT MATTERS FOR THE CONDITION SWEEP: this claim is OUTCOME-phrased and still wrong — which is the MORE dangerous variant, because outcome-phrasing makes a claim CHECKABLE and therefore invites trust.** ⚠ **There was no ⛔ warning-comment attached to it either** — the tell that flagged `§ARM-RESEARCH` was absent here. **Amends contracts L131: outcome-phrasing buys CHECKABILITY, not TRUTH.**
**Done-when:** `projectSyncResult` carries `healthSignal`/its reason through the `"advanced"` branch, `connectorSyncHealthWorkflow` surfaces it, **and a test constructs `status:"advanced"` WITH a signal present and asserts it reaches the health sink** — ⛔ **the test that does not enter the branch is what let this ship.** *(origin: 2026-08-11, 24.6 round 4, partition ACC-1)*

### 24.25 — `main-bundle-resolution.test.ts` fails at round seal; cause not established, and it is UNOWNED (origin: 24.12 session-end preflight)
- [x] DONE — re-scoped and closed `111506a6` 2026-08-11 (main-orchestrator) · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** nothing further

⚠ **CAUSE NOT ESTABLISHED — pre-existing vs environmental (a toolchain/subprocess failure) has NOT been distinguished, and the difference decides who owns it.** ⛔ **Do not close this by re-running it once and seeing green: an intermittent build subprocess and a real regression are indistinguishable on a single pass** (contracts **L111**: two runs disagreeing means FLAKY, never PASSING).
⭐ **RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THIS ROUND'S OWN HEADLINE IN MINIATURE: the failure WAS caught — by an implementer running the full gate at close-out, in another area's territory, and flagging rather than fixing. Whether it PROPAGATES into a tracked task was the only thing at risk, and that is precisely the class this round diagnosed.**
⛔⛔ **SECOND OBSERVATION, AND THE TWO IMPLEMENTERS CLASSIFIED IT DIFFERENTLY — THAT DIVERGENCE IS THE MORE USEFUL FINDING.** providers-integrations hit the **same red** on its own monorepo-wide `/preflight` and reported it as **observed-not-verified, *"the documented false-positive signature"* per contracts **L83*** — a repo-wide run taken while OTHER implementers hold uncommitted WIP — **and deliberately did not re-chase it.** knowledge, running the same gate minutes earlier, **flagged it as a failure to be tracked.** ⇒ ⭐ **ONE OBSERVATION, TWO DISPOSITIONS — and only one of them became a task.** ⚠ **Both were behaving correctly by their own lights, which is what makes it worth recording: the disposition depended on whether the runner knew L83's signature, not on anything about the failure.**
⚠ **THIS DOES NOT CLOSE THE TASK, AND MUST NOT BE READ AS CLOSING IT.** ⭐ **It does materially re-order what a successor checks FIRST: the L83 concurrent-WIP false-positive hypothesis is now the leading one** — consistent with the measured zero source changes to any desktop bundle input — **but *consistent with* is not *established*, and a second observer reaching for a known signature is not a reproduction.** ⛔ **Re-run it on a CLEAN tree with no concurrent WIP. That single condition distinguishes the two hypotheses and nothing else does.**
**Done-when:** the failure is reproduced or shown environmental, and either fixed or recorded as a known-flaky with its discriminator. *(origin: 2026-08-11, knowledge-implementer session-end preflight)*
⭐ **RE-SCOPED AND CLOSED 2026-08-11 (lead's discriminating run + orchestrator ruling, per handoff instruction — "the disposition is yours to rule").** The lead ran the Done-when's own discriminator: **three clean-tree runs of `main-bundle-resolution.test.ts`, all PASS (4/4).** Combined with the already-measured zero-source-diff to any bundle input, **the L83 concurrent-WIP false-positive explanation is now ESTABLISHED, not merely consistent** — Done-when's first clause ("shown environmental") is satisfied. ⛔ **But per the handoff's own instruction, the test result does not close this task — the test was never the finding.** The finding is the SECOND observation (`:3122`): two implementers hit the identical failure minutes apart and disposed of it differently — one filed it, one recognized L83's signature and skipped it — and **that divergence survives the test going green.** ⇒ **Re-scoped to what was actually load-bearing: banked as `packages/contracts/LESSONS.md#136`** — *a documented false-positive signature is a citation, not a silent skip*: the fix is not "stop recognizing L83," it is "say so when you do," so a future reader isn't left re-deriving whether the check happened. With the environmental cause established AND the process gap captured as an enforceable-on-review convention, nothing further is owed on this task; **closed, not by the test passing, but by the divergence being named and given a durable home.**

4. **An explicit check of whether any existing dev/prod database has been running WITHOUT the `task` table — the answer RECORDED, not assumed.** ⚠ **This is the only leg with real-world consequences: it determines whether the gap is theoretical or has already bitten someone silently.** ⛔ **Not urgent tonight; must not evaporate in the cycle.**

**Kind:** fix (tooling) · **Spec:** — (process/tooling) · **Files:** a git `pre-commit` hook or a `.claude` PreToolUse guard · `scripts/plan-lint.sh` (unchanged, already correct)
⛔ **THE FINDING, RE-MEASURED AND NARROWED — the original claim was FALSE and the residual is real.** `(a0)(v)` said *"`plan-lint` has no equivalent trigger ANYWHERE."* **Measured 2026-08-12: false** — `.claude/commands/orchestrate-end.md:100` runs it and `:175` forbids committing over a non-zero exit. ⭐ **The real defect is narrower: that gates the ROUND-CLOSE commit, not the per-edit HOT-WRITE**, and the routing matrix directs the orchestrator to write this file *hot*, many times per round, between close-outs.
⭐⭐ **THE EVIDENCE IS THE ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN, AND IT IS THE ARGUMENT: on 2026-08-12 `plan-lint` caught THREE separate violations in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` in one session** — a state token in a task heading (`24.39`), a `DONE` line missing its backticked hash (`24.40`), and a resolved Carry-forward item **annotated in place** (`24.41` — the exact rule that task's own Done-when quotes). ⛔ **Each was committed by the person who had just written the rule being broken.** ⇒ ***an orchestrator who "knows the conventions" is precisely who this catches***, so the mitigation cannot be knowledge.
⛔ **INTERIM MITIGATION IS ALREADY IN PLACE, and it is NOT this task:** `docs/orchestrator-briefing.md`'s Step-9 routing section now carries a mandatory before-and-after `plan-lint` line (landed with this filing). ⚠ **That is a documented habit — per `L110` a mitigation recorded as an option is not a mitigation, and per `L109` a check read after the action is a receipt, not a gate.** **This task is the STRUCTURAL form.**
⭐⭐ **LEAD RULING 2026-08-12 — BUILD IT, SHIP IT NOT INSTALLED.** The script lands in-repo with a documented one-line install command and is **NOT wired into `.git/hooks/` for anyone**. ⇒ **the machinery exists, verified and tested; ACTIVATION is a deliberate act by whoever owns the working copy.** ⭐ **This is the project's own built · dormant · armed-by-choice posture applied to its own tooling — there is no reason it should get a weaker treatment than a connector.** **It also dissolves the sign-off question: nothing changes anyone's commit behaviour until they opt in.**
**Done-when — all three:**
1. A tracker edit **cannot reach a commit ungated** once installed: the hook runs `scripts/plan-lint.sh` and **fails the commit** on a non-zero exit, so the violation is unrepresentable rather than detected (`L103`).
2. ⚠ **Must not fire on commits that don't touch `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`** — a gate that cries wolf gets disabled, and a disabled gate protects nothing (`L89`).
3. ⭐ **Independently runnable as a plain command** (`bash scripts/hooks/pre-commit-plan-lint.sh`). ⛔ **A guard that exists ONLY inside a git hook cannot be tested, cannot be lifted into CI later, and cannot be verified by anyone who has not installed it.**
⛔ **ACCEPTED RESIDUAL, recorded rather than papered over: a git hook is REPO-LOCAL and is NOT shared by clone.** It protects one working copy — not teammates, not CI, not a fresh clone. ⇒ ⛔ **the mandatory before-and-after `plan-lint` line in `docs/orchestrator-briefing.md` REMAINS BINDING and must NOT be deleted when this lands.** ⭐ **They are COMPLEMENTS: the hook is opt-in, so the doc line is the only thing binding on an orchestrator who has not installed it.**
⭐ **DONE `27a65ecb`'s successor — VERIFIED EMPIRICALLY BY THE THREE-STATE PROTOCOL (`L75`), not asserted:** **(1)** tracker not staged → skips, exit **0** · **(2)** clean tracker, forced → PASS, exit **0** · **(3)** deliberate violation injected (`DONE` token added to a task heading) → **exit 1** with the `BLOCKED` message. **File restored and confirmed byte-identical (empty `git diff`, `plan-lint` back to 0 violations).** ⚠ **Mutation window kept to seconds per `L139`** (a reviewer's break-then-restore is unsafe in a shared non-worktree checkout). ⚠ **The first exit-code reading came back EMPTY because it was taken through a pipe — re-measured without one (`L111`: never report an exit code obtained through a pipe).** *(origin: 2026-08-12, 24.41's re-measurement; lead-ruled built-dormant the same day)*

### 24.43 — `lifecycle.test.ts`'s hand-maintained `DOMAIN_TABLES` array is the SAME stale-enumeration anti-pattern `24.39` just fixed, in a second file — plus `24.39`'s detector is one-directional

- [x] **DONE `a5214c8e`** 2026-08-12 (worker-implementer) — filed the same day (main-orchestrator, from `24.39`'s Step 9; **surfaced by worker-implementer AND its code-quality reviewer independently**) · **Depends:** `24.39` (its barrel-derived detector is what supersedes leg A) · **Blocks:** — · **Track:** worker (`packages/db`)
**Kind:** fix (cleanup + hardening) · **Spec:** §4 · **Files:** `packages/db/test/migrate/lifecycle.test.ts:67-92` (`DOMAIN_TABLES`) · `packages/db/test/migrate/schema-migration-coverage.test.ts` (`24.39`'s detector)
⭐ **LEG A — the stale list.** `DOMAIN_TABLES` is a **hand-maintained array** that had **ALREADY drifted before `24.39` touched anything**: missing `parity_reports`, `write_receipts`, `health_items`, `schedule_bookkeeping`, `instance_leases` — **and `task`, until `24.39`.** ⛔ **This is precisely the anti-pattern `24.39` exists to eliminate, sitting in a second file in the same directory**, and it is why `24.39`'s brief insisted the new detector derive its set from the schema barrel rather than a literal array (`L61`: when a finding names one call site, look for the CONSTRUCTION — the second copy is the one that ships). ⚠ **It is not actively WRONG — it is an incomplete subset-check that happens to still pass**, which is the more dangerous shape: it reports coverage of a set it does not enumerate.
**LEG B — the one-directional detector.** `24.39`'s `schema-migration-coverage.test.ts` asserts **schema ⊆ migrated**. **The mirror defect — a migration creating a table the schema no longer declares — would go unnoticed.** ⭐ **Deferred WITH A MEASUREMENT, not with a hope: worker hand-verified the sqlite side that the migrated set EQUALS the declared set (23 = 23, zero orphans) at `24.39`'s commit.** ⇒ **the gap is future drift, not a live orphan.**
**Done-when:** `DOMAIN_TABLES` is **deleted in favour of the barrel-derived enumeration** (or, if it is kept for a reason the deleter can state, that reason is written in-file and the array is completed and pinned so it cannot silently drift again); **AND** the detector is made **bidirectional** (declared set == migrated set, both directions, both dialects), so an orphaned migration is a failure rather than a hand-check someone remembered to run once. *(origin: 2026-08-12, 24.39 Step 9, worker-implementer + code-quality-reviewer independently)*

### 24.44 — `serveProjection`'s re-gate denial also needs its audit persisted — the cross-track half `24.33` deliberately did NOT take

- [ ] OPEN — filed 2026-08-12 (main-orchestrator, from `24.33`'s Step 2.5; ⭐ **the design argument is knowledge-implementer's and is recorded here in their framing so it is not rediscovered**) · **Depends:** `24.33` (the `GclAuditPersistPort` + `persistDenialAudit` mechanism it builds) · **Blocks:** — (dormant, like `24.33`) · **Track:** ⚠ **knowledge + worker — a genuine producer/consumer PAIR**
**Kind:** fix (completes `24.33`'s coverage) · **Spec:** §5, §16 · Safety rule 4 · **Files:** `packages/knowledge/src/gcl/projection.ts` (`serveProjection` sync → async, threading `auditPersist`) · `apps/worker/src/composition/crossWorkspaceRead.ts:139` (**one word: `await`**)
⭐ **THE ARGUMENT FOR DOING IT AT ALL, knowledge-implementer's, and it is the stronger half of the pair:** `serveProjection`'s re-gate denial is the **defense-in-depth catch of a post-write tamper** — **arguably the MORE safety-critical audit case of the two**, since `admitAndPersistProjection` catches a bad projection on the way IN while `serveProjection` catches one that is already stored. ⇒ **covering only the write path leaves the more important denial silent.**
⛔ **WHY `24.33` SCOPED IT OUT rather than taking it: `serveProjection` has a PRODUCTION caller outside `packages/knowledge`** — `crossWorkspaceRead.ts:139` calls it **synchronously** (`const admitted = serveProjection(...)`, then `isErr(admitted)` and `admitted.value` in a per-row loop) ⇒ **making it async breaks that file's compile.** ⚠ **`L121` covers a widening's own compile-break in another area's TEST FIXTURE; this is PRODUCTION code, so applying it here would be a first-of-its-kind widening of a standing rule — and `L121`'s own provenance says that arrives as a REQUEST to the lead, not an orchestrator authorizing unilaterally.**
⭐ **SIZING, established not estimated: the worker-side change is ONE WORD.** `resolveApprovedCrossWorkspaceSlice` is **already `async`** (`crossWorkspaceRead.ts:81`, already 3 `await`s) ⇒ the fix is `const admitted = await serveProjection(...)`. **No cascade.**
⭐ **AND WHY DEFERRING COSTS NOTHING REAL: `24.33`'s whole path is DORMANT** — neither function has a production caller — **so "the more critical case stays silent" is true of a FUTURE state either way. Nothing is exposed for one minute longer by splitting this.**
**Done-when:** `serveProjection` threads the same `auditPersist` port and persists its denial audits through the **same `persistDenialAudit` helper `24.33` built** (⛔ **not a second copy of the `isRedactionSafe` gate** — `L39`); `crossWorkspaceRead.ts:139` awaits it; **the repo compiles green in ONE landing** (⚠ **producer and consumer must not be split across a red window — if they land as two commits, they land in the same round and the second is not optional**); and `serveProjection`'s deny path gets the same four pins `24.33` established (carries-the-signal · end-to-end durable record · fail-closed on unsafe · **allow persists nothing**). *(origin: 2026-08-12, 24.33 Step 2.5, knowledge-implementer)*

### 24.45 — ⚠ `isRedactionSafe` is a keyword/prefix HEURISTIC whose own doc claims "no representable violation today" — and `MALFORMED_POLICY_INPUT` embeds an UNVALIDATED `workspaceId` it never audited

- [ ] OPEN — filed 2026-08-12 (main-orchestrator, from `24.33`'s Step 9; **found by knowledge-implementer's `security-reviewer`, routed rather than absorbed**) · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** ⛔ **Phase 25.2/25.4's port wiring — this must be settled BEFORE a real `GclAuditPersistPort` binds** · **Track:** providers-integrations (`packages/policy`)
**Kind:** fix (safety) · **Spec:** §5, §16 · **Safety rules 7 (redaction) with a rule-4 flavour** · **Files:** `packages/policy/src/audit-signal.ts` (`isRedactionSafe`)
⛔ **THE FINDING:** `isRedactionSafe` is a **keyword/prefix/URL-userinfo heuristic, not a shape allowlist**, and **its own doc comment asserts "no representable violation… today" on the basis of an audit of existing producers.** ⭐ **That audit missed a producer: `validateProjectionVisibility`'s `MALFORMED_POLICY_INPUT` path embeds the candidate's OWN, UNVALIDATED `workspaceId` into `audit.refs` BEFORE confirming it matches the real source workspace.** ⇒ **a sensitive-but-not-credential-shaped `workspaceId` passes the heuristic today.**
⭐⭐ **THE TIMING IS THE SHARP PART, AND IT IS WHY THIS BLOCKS RATHER THAN WAITS: it is harmless PRECISELY BECAUSE nothing is wired — and it becomes DURABLY PERSISTED the moment Phase 25.2/25.4 binds a real port.** ⛔ **The defect ARRIVES WITH THE FIX FOR IT.** ⚠ **A wiring slice that reads "24.33 already handles the audit" would ship it.**
⚠ **This is `L96`'s shape** (a claim of coverage in a doc comment, unverified against a producer the author didn't enumerate) **and `L123`'s** (a heuristic standing where a gate is assumed). ⭐ **`24.33` deliberately placed its `isRedactionSafe` call in `packages/knowledge`'s own `persistDenialAudit` rather than inside the future port — so the gate is REACHED; this task is about whether the gate is RIGHT.**
**Done-when:** either `isRedactionSafe` becomes a **shape allowlist** rather than a keyword heuristic for the fields it admits, **or** the `MALFORMED_POLICY_INPUT` producer stops embedding an unvalidated `workspaceId` in `audit.refs` (validate-or-omit), **or** the doc comment's "no representable violation" claim is **retracted and replaced with the enumerated producer set it actually rests on** (`L82`: make the claim true or withdraw it — never re-point it at handy evidence). ⚠ **Whichever route: the producer enumeration must be RE-DERIVED, not inherited — the existing one is what missed this.** *(origin: 2026-08-12, 24.33 Step 9, knowledge-implementer's security-reviewer)*

### 24.46 — The "canonical" raw-content key set is authoritative by DESIGNATION, not by CONSTRUCTION — nobody has asked whether its keys are complete

- [ ] OPEN — filed 2026-08-12 (main-orchestrator, from `24.32`'s Step 2.5; **the finding is worker-implementer's, surfaced by actually reading both implementations rather than assuming the fork was a subset**) · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** — · **Track:** contract (`packages/contracts`)
**Kind:** audit (+ likely fix) · **Spec:** §16 (candidate-data gate) · Safety rule 4 · **Files:** `packages/contracts/src/models/gcl-projection.ts` (`RAW_CONTENT_SHAPED_KEYS` / `isRawContentShaped`)
⛔ **THE FINDING, and it is bigger than the key that revealed it:** `24.32`'s fork carried the key **`"notebody"`**, which the canonical set does **NOT** contain (it is an **exact-match** Set — `"note"`/`"notes"` do not cover it). ⇒ ⭐ **the canonical set was never a strict superset of the fork, which proves the two lists were INDEPENDENTLY MAINTAINED.** ⛔ **"Canonical" is authoritative by DESIGNATION, not by construction — its 14 keys are the ones its author thought of, and nobody has ever asked whether that enumeration is complete.**
⭐ **WHY `24.34`'s SWEEP COULD NOT HAVE FOUND THIS, which is the reusable part: `24.34` hunted forks of the TRAVERSAL — reimplementations of the walk. This is a gap in the KEY SET the walk consults.** ⚠ **A predicate can be perfectly single-sourced and still be wrong, and a sweep for duplicates is structurally blind to it** (`L118`: the property measured was *"is there another implementation?"*, not *"is the implementation right?"*).
⛔⛔ **SHARPENED 2026-08-12 by `24.32`'s SECURITY-REVIEWER, and it WEAKENS the justification the orchestrator approved — recorded because the correction runs upward:** `24.32`'s narrowing was accepted on three legs, the third being *"the value-shape check is primary, so the loss only bites a short single-line value under that exact key."* ⭐ **That leg carried a hidden premise — that we know what a real payload looks like. WE DO NOT: there is NO real `SchedulingProjection` implementation anywhere in the repo. Every `d.payload` today is a TEST FIXTURE.** ⇒ **"no real payload would carry `notebody`" is currently true VACUOUSLY, not because a producer was audited.** ⭐ **THE ACTIONABLE CONSEQUENCE, and it is this task's most concrete line: whoever builds the real `SchedulingProjection` producer MUST re-check its emitted key vocabulary against the canonical denylist** — the check cannot be done now because the thing to check does not exist. ⚠ **`L84`'s two-readings shape: the guard is not unasserted, it is UNREACHED — and those demand different fixes.**
⚠ **`"notebody"` itself is NOT the issue and must not be treated as the fix:** measured 2026-08-12, it appears **exactly once repo-wide** (the fork's own list), no producer emits it, and `24.32` accepted its loss as a recorded narrowing on three stated legs. ⛔ **Adding one key back would close the instance and leave the question — which is this project's signature failure.**
**Done-when:** the key set is **DERIVED from something** rather than enumerated from memory — a documented rule for what belongs (⚠ the value-shape check is the primary gate, so the key list is defence-in-depth and its completeness bar should be stated, not assumed), **or** the enumeration is explicitly declared best-effort with that limitation written where consumers read it (`L82`: make the claim true or withdraw it). ⚠ **Enumerate by CONCEPT, not by scanning for more plausible-looking key names** (`L64`) — the question is *what makes a key raw-content-shaped*, not *which words did we forget*. *(origin: 2026-08-12, 24.32 Step 2.5, worker-implementer)*

## Phase 26 — Research/Web-Retrieval Provider Go-Live + Living-Vault Scheduling
**Goal:** Arm the built-and-dormant RES-1 research provider (real Perplexity/xAI transport + paid key) so `/research` + `/research-deep` reach live cloud endpoints — every call egress-veto-gated (employer-work ack-OFF ⇒ fail closed, no cloud fallback) — and register the dormant living-vault synthesis (KN-10) into the §19.12 scheduled bundle so the vault rewrites itself on a cadence. Posture: HARD-LINE (crossing 8, INDEPENDENT arc — owner confirm per crossing; two standing hard lines: real external fetch + paid-key provisioning); gated only on Phase 17 (Keychain). · **Spec anchors:** §19.13 (activation home); §7 (RES-1/RES-2 provider + broker route); §5 (egress veto — research is a cloud processor, query text employer-content-bearing); §6 (KN-10/KN-11/KN-12 — synthesis via KnowledgeWriter, backlinks derived); §9 (register + schedule researchDeep + livingVaultSynthesis, LIFE-2); §19.4 (paid key via SecretsPort); §11 (surfaces) · **Track:** providers-integrations, knowledge · **Depends on (phases):** 13 (RES-1 + synthesis dormant build — 13.13/13.14/13.8a–e), 17 (Keychain §ARM-17)
**Gate:** — (no /phase-exit run)

### 26.1 — Research provider go-live (real Perplexity/xAI transport + paid key)
- [ ] OWNER-GATED ⛔ Owner-Gates §ARM-RESEARCH
**Kind:** build+crossing · **Spec:** §19.13 / §7 (RES-1) / §5 / §19.4 · **Depends:** 13.13, 13.14, 17 (§ARM-17 Keychain) · **Blocks:** —
**Files:** `packages/providers/src/model-provider/research-provider.ts` (real transport bind) · `apps/worker/src/composition/backends.ts` (research egress-processor allow-entry)
Per §ARM-RESEARCH steps 1–4: provision `providers/perplexity` + `providers/xai` paid keys (Keychain), confirm the real `/chat/completions` + `/v1/responses` wire shapes against a live call, bind the real `createPerplexityTransport`/`createGrokLiveSearchTransport` behind the SecretsAccessor, add the research processor to `SOW_EGRESS_ALLOWED_PROCESSORS` (each vendor its OWN id, never an alias — rule 5), flip the default-OFF strict-`=== true` research arm. Employer-work ack-OFF fails closed by construction; rollback = 1 flag. **Done-when:** a live `/research` + `/research-deep` run returns cited findings → candidate → KnowledgeWriter note, and an employer-work ack-OFF run fails closed at the live endpoint.

### 26.2 — Living-vault synthesis scheduling (register + arm the KN-10 activity)
- [ ] OWNER-GATED ⛔ Owner-Gates §ARM-RESEARCH
**Kind:** build+crossing · **Spec:** §19.13 / §6 (KN-10) / §9 (schedule/LIFE-2) · **Depends:** 13.8e · **Blocks:** —
**Files:** `apps/worker/src/temporal/` (register `livingVaultSynthesis` into the §19.12 bundle + schedule)
Per §ARM-RESEARCH step 5: register the dormant `livingVaultSynthesis` activity (13.8e) into the §19.12 scheduled bundle behind its own default-OFF strict-`=== true` arming flag, with LIFE-2 catch-up collapse. Additive writes AUTO, human-relevant edits PROPOSE; digest + one-undo. **Done-when:** an armed schedule runs the synthesis pass on a cadence (additive auto, human-relevant proposed), default boot registers no schedule, and rollback is one flag.

### Acceptance criteria (26)
- [ ] OPEN — both 26.X unticked; to close: a live `/research`+`/research-deep` run reaches the real Perplexity/xAI endpoints and lands cited findings as a candidate → KnowledgeWriter note (never a direct write); an employer-work ack-OFF run fails closed at the live endpoint (rule 5, no cloud fallback); each vendor is its own egress processor id (never an alias); the living-vault synthesis runs on an armed schedule (additive AUTO, human-relevant PROPOSE, digest + one-undo) and default boot registers no schedule; both arms are one-flag reversible.

## Decisions tabled

- **Schema-snapshot coverage scope** — RESOLVED at the contract layer 2026-06-30 (all 27 Appendix-A seam models covered); the residual decision survives only for downstream-phase artifacts (archive Part 1).
- **Config-default deferrals** (Phase-0 config, not contract changes): LIFE-2 catch-up window · LIFE-1 lease TTL/heartbeat + fencing-token location · crash-loop threshold · backup cadence · default `maxCostUsd` · per-preset composition. Built configurable with documented defaults; confirm before V1 ship.
- **GBrain live 4-condition round-trip validation → Phase 12 (12.7/12.22/12.23)** — the enablement gate for the per-workspace `writeThroughEnabled` flip (decision itself resolved, ADR-007; only the live proof is deferred). Related: the **gbrain pin-identity sub-decision** (SHA vs semver — gbrain exposes no local commit-SHA; task 11.3 + §ARM-GBRAIN step 3).
- **Local-provider conformance (Ollama / LM Studio)** — operator-deferred; both stay `conformanceStatus: unknown` (matrix-ineligible). Architecturally required as the §5 Employer-Work zero-egress route — validate before any local egress path ships.
- **Native-key provider conformance (Anthropic/OpenAI direct)** — extraction now runs Option-B subscription (§ARM-18), so native-key conformance stays deferred; OpenRouter routes live-verified. Per-token pricing for OpenAI/OpenRouter: `costEstimation.pricing` null-with-cited-source; operator fills.
- **Signed + notarized DMG (hardened runtime)** — V1.1 (§15); unsigned build-from-source proven for V1.
- **§DEC-CAT4 — FailureClass security members** (see Owner-Gates; frozen-contract, owner's call).
- **Workspace reclassification path (owner-gated — recorded 2026-07-15, lead ruling).** Workspace `type`/isolation-class and every workspace-binding anchor (`workspace_type_immutable`, `project_workspace_immutable`, `connector_instance_workspace_immutable` — 14.1/14.6/14.2, worker L30) are **immutable-by-default as the safe floor**. The ONLY escape hatch is a deliberate, owner-gated, audited **reclassification** operation that re-scopes/re-indexes the resource (rule 4/5, §5/§19.1) — a future session must NEVER satisfy a rebind need by weakening the immutability guard in place.
- **Accepted redaction residual (§16 boundary).** A secret mislabeled under a system id field (`correlationId`/`*Id`/`*Ref`) passes the id-charset gate — an accepted, recorded boundary of the redaction design (phase-10 exit), not an open defect.
- **FailureVariant→FailureClass collapse (recorded).** `validation_rejected`+`schema_rejected` collapse to `schema_rejection`; `degraded_unavailable`→`worker_down`. Deliberate taxonomy mapping — cross-linked to §DEC-CAT4 + Carry-forward #5 (open enum members).
